# Model Plane and the Verevon chat and agent pages: audit of Claude, Hermes, DeepSeek harness, T3 Code, Manus, Codex, Antigravity, Cursor and the web chat products

*As of 2026-10-14. Scope: the Model Plane (Rust and Go) and the Verevon chat and agent pages in the Frontend Plane, compared with eight installed or open-source reference tools and four public web chat products. Read-only audit; nothing in the product or the reference apps was changed.*

## How to read this document

**Tiers.** Every recommendation sits in one of three tiers.

- **PRIORITY** = do first: gaps that block or visibly undercut the vision, table-stakes capabilities every comparable reference has and Verevon lacks or half has, real safety, security or data-governance risks, and high-leverage unique capabilities with acceptable effort.
- **RECOMMENDED** = clear evidenced value that supports the vision but does not block it, larger efforts that need sequencing, and differentiators worth adopting after the priority set.
- **NICE TO HAVE** = polish, niche, speculative or modest value.

**Provenance tags.** Every factual statement about a reference carries a tag: [REA] reverse-engineered with REA (the reverse-engineering analysis toolset) from the shipped app; [SRC] read in source code or shipped files; [DOC] documentation only, public or internal; [INF] inference, with the basis stated. The four web products (claude.ai, chatgpt.com, perplexity.ai, manus.im) rest on public documentation only, so their claims are [DOC] unless stated otherwise.

**Status vocabulary for "Verevon today".** Absent (nothing in code), Partial (some of the capability exists in code), Stub (a placeholder or dark module exists), Planned (a plan or decision exists, no code). "Shipped" appears only in the strengths and common-ground sections. Effort uses S, M, L and XL. Waves W1 to W5 are the build order set out in section 9. "OD" numbers refer to the open decisions in the chat gap-closure plan (CHAT_GAP_CLOSURE_PLAN_2026-10-01.md). Ask, Plan and Do are Verevon's answer, planning and acting chat modes.

**Ids and anchors.** Recommendations are numbered F-t3-001 to F-t3-098, ordered by tier and rank. Each has an anchor with the same name, so [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001) jumps to its card. Prevalence is written "n of N references", where N is normally 12, the number of reference sources in this audit; categories that could be assessed on fewer sources say so (11 for multimodal and research, 9 for coding). Numbering follows the pre-verification tier order, so F-t3-016 keeps its number after moving to Recommended.

**Verification.** Every "Verevon today" status, gap description and effort was verified by reading the Verevon code at commit afb83928 (2026-10-10). Every Priority item was also re-checked on the reference side by a second, independent pass that re-opened the cited evidence and tried to refute it. Where verification changed a claim, the verified version is the one printed here; Appendix C lists the corrections and Appendix D the tier and effort changes.

## 1. Executive summary

### Goal

Verevon's chat and agent pages aim to be one web product that combines what manus.im, perplexity.ai, chatgpt.com and claude.ai offer: chat, code, images, research, computer use and connected apps, all driven by the Model Plane. This audit compares that target against the Claude desktop app (the overall reference to be re-created in Rust), Hermes Agent (memory and self-tailoring), the DeepSeek harness (transparency and customisation), T3 Code (clean multi-model UI), Manus (computer control), Codex (plugins and ChatGPT-model capabilities), Antigravity (Gemini capabilities) and Cursor (model harnessing, not IDE features), plus the four web products. It answers which capabilities every reference shares, who implements each best, what each does uniquely well, and where Verevon stands. The output is 98 recommendations, tiered, sequenced into waves and tied to the architecture decisions they need.

### Where Verevon stands today

The code-verified baseline holds 500 capability records across 13 categories: 290 shipped, 122 partial, 57 absent, 9 stub and 22 planned [SRC]. The picture is uneven. The strongest categories are Transparency, observability and control (26 of 33 shipped, 2 absent), Chat experience (56 of 77 shipped), Safety, permissions and governance (25 of 37 shipped, nothing absent) and Research and web (22 of 34 shipped, nothing absent). Verevon already matches the references on the chat substrate: streaming, rendering, web search with citations, context compaction, subagents, typed contracts and identity. Its signed fail-closed capability gate, autonomy ladder, Zero Data Retention (ZDR) design and credential isolation are stronger than any reference.

The weakest categories are Computer and environment control (11 of 31 shipped, 9 absent), Coding capability (4 of 10 shipped), Multimodal and creation (18 of 41 shipped, 16 partial) and Models and routing (25 of 41 shipped, 10 absent). Agent runtime is the largest category in the reference data (420 records) and the most unfinished in practice: 43 of 77 shipped but 22 partial, with the gaps sitting in the loop itself. A recurring pattern across categories is "built, but dark": finished or nearly finished code that is not deployed, not wired or not proven.

### Headline findings

1. **The agent loop is the product, and it is not finished.** Plain chat is close to parity, but Do runs start without their instruction stack, history and memory, cannot resume in place after a pause, cannot ask the user a typed question and cannot run on a schedule with tools. See [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001), [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002), [F-t3-011](#F-t3-011), [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018).
2. **A large share of the gaps are already built but dark.** The audit outbox fix is built and not deployed, the ZDR design is complete pending one attestation, MCP (Model Context Protocol) calls are refused by the gate and connector reads are approval-gated as if they were writes, browser grants are placeholders and the OpenAI cache order is built and off. Finishing beats building. See [F-t3-004](#F-t3-004), [F-t3-005](#F-t3-005), [F-t3-010](#F-t3-010), [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003), [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008), [F-t3-022](#F-t3-022).
3. **Governed autonomy is the differentiator, but the declarative layer users and admins see is missing.** Declared tool side-effect classes, scoped approvals with cause codes, named modes and an org policy registry, stored consent for unattended runs and per-site browser policy are what the references show in parts (driving approvals from a declared side-effect class is a Verevon synthesis) and Verevon lacks. See [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003), [F-t3-006](#F-t3-006), [F-t3-032](#F-t3-032), [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018).
4. **The conversation data model is thinner than in the leading references.** Message versions and forks, full-text search, agent recall of past chats, paging, projects on top of Spaces and sharing are partial or absent. See [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012), [F-t3-013](#F-t3-013), [F-t3-046](#F-t3-046).
5. **The path to "acts like a PC" runs in steps.** A governed agent browser first, then a per-thread sandbox computer behind a backend seam, then computer use inside that sandbox; user-desktop control stays parked. See [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008), [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009), [F-t3-035](#F-t3-035), [F-t3-024](#F-t3-024).
6. **Verevon learns already, but without review, scope or undo.** Manus stages learned skills as proposals the user accepts or rejects, and Hermes stages unattended memory edits (its skill approval gate is opt-in and off by default); Verevon's loop writes org-matchable skills while the promotion gate is deliberately unbuilt. Governance first, then tailoring. See [F-t3-007](#F-t3-007), [F-t3-020](#F-t3-020), [F-t3-061](#F-t3-061).
7. **Deliverables, not only answers.** Verified Office files through executable SKILL.md bundles, decks, an editable canvas and research cited to passages are where the web products and Claude desktop set the bar (Office skills are table-stakes; decks, the canvas and passage-level citations are common or differentiators). See [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014), [F-t3-038](#F-t3-038), [F-t3-031](#F-t3-031), [F-t3-053](#F-t3-053).
8. **Nobody owns model entitlements.** model-gateway refuses the OrgPolicy allowed_models field because no service owns it; Control Plane should decide and Model Plane enforce. See [F-t3-023](#F-t3-023), [F-t3-065](#F-t3-065).
9. **Verevon is honest by contract but not yet visible.** Live cost with a pre-run estimate, typed lifecycle events, readable long runs and a trajectory ledger are where DeepSeek, Manus and Antigravity lead. See [F-t3-028](#F-t3-028), [F-t3-049](#F-t3-049), [F-t3-048](#F-t3-048), [F-t3-034](#F-t3-034).
10. **Open standards keep Verevon in the ecosystem the references share.** SKILL.md, MCP that executes, MCP Apps, plugins with supply-chain pins and an outward MCP endpoint. See [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014), [F-t3-045](#F-t3-045), [F-t3-059](#F-t3-059), [F-t3-089](#F-t3-089).

### Decisions leadership must make

Ten topics need an ADR (Architecture Decision Record) or an owner ruling before implementation starts, because they touch standing Verevon rules: plane ownership, ZDR propagation, Space authority, "the Model proposes and Quarry executes" and "no LLM on the permission hot path".

1. Sandbox computer backend and thread-scoped workspace: microVM per thread, snapshot retention and destruction, cost metering ([F-t3-009](#F-t3-009), [F-t3-024](#F-t3-024), [F-t3-035](#F-t3-035), [F-t3-043](#F-t3-043)).
2. Computer-use scope: desktop control only inside Verevon's sandbox VM; user-desktop control stays parked ([F-t3-024](#F-t3-024), [F-t3-026](#F-t3-026)).
3. Spaces as the chat project container, instruction precedence and multi-author authority, open decision OD19 ([F-t3-013](#F-t3-013), [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001), [F-t3-060](#F-t3-060), [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012)).
4. Agent-browser domain policy ownership and model-blind credentials, OD13 ([F-t3-008](#F-t3-008), [F-t3-026](#F-t3-026), [F-t3-050](#F-t3-050)).
5. Unattended authority for scheduled, goal and trigger runs: stored consent with expiry and renewable delegated credentials, owner ruling 2026-09-28, OD8, D10 ([F-t3-018](#F-t3-018), [F-t3-037](#F-t3-037), [F-t3-047](#F-t3-047), [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002)).
6. Share model, public links and artifact hosting origin under ZDR and residency ([F-t3-046](#F-t3-046), [F-t3-045](#F-t3-045), [F-t3-031](#F-t3-031)).
7. Learned and distributed behaviour: a three-tier skill library (vendor, org, user), the promotion gate and the plugin supply chain ([F-t3-007](#F-t3-007), [F-t3-058](#F-t3-058), [F-t3-059](#F-t3-059), [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014)).
8. Model entitlements and provider credential ownership: allowed_models has no owner and bring-your-own keys need a custodian ([F-t3-023](#F-t3-023), [F-t3-065](#F-t3-065), [F-t3-042](#F-t3-042)).
9. LLM reviewers on the permission path: only a tighten-only design (deny or escalate, never grant) could be compatible with the standing rule ([F-t3-097](#F-t3-097)).
10. Connector content in shared Spaces and ZDR propagation for connector and web reads ([F-t3-010](#F-t3-010), [F-t3-056](#F-t3-056), [F-t3-027](#F-t3-027), [F-t3-055](#F-t3-055)).

The open questions with the widest effect: which provider surface is attested first for ZDR and when Z-40 closes commercially; self-hosted microVMs or an attested managed provider for the sandbox computer, scoped to the thread, the Space or both; whether user-desktop control is permanently out of scope or only parked; how delegated credentials are renewed for runs that pause for hours (OD8); whether Ask mode may read connected apps (F12); whether Vertex AI in an EU region meets the residency and ZDR bar, which gates the Gemini family ([F-t3-041](#F-t3-041)); when Do mode is enabled on the product route (OD2); and when the Channel Plane moves from docs-only to build.

### What changed during verification

Verification covered all 98 recommendations. Before it, the tiers held 23 Priority, 45 Recommended and 30 Nice-to-have items; after it, 22, 46 and 30. The one tier change is [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016), moved from Priority to Recommended because the Redis buffer, drain and durable receipts already exist and the item is a scale-readiness hardening rather than a precondition for durable runs. Verdicts were keep for 13 items, reword for 81 and demote for 4; no item was dropped as already built and none remains unverified or low-confidence. Effort was revised on 40 items, mostly upward, because the code showed bundles that span several planes ([F-t3-008](#F-t3-008), [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014) and [F-t3-037](#F-t3-037) went to XL) and sometimes downward where parts already exist ([F-t3-026](#F-t3-026), [F-t3-044](#F-t3-044), [F-t3-066](#F-t3-066)). The verified Verevon status across the 98 items is 78 Partial, 16 Absent, 2 Stub and 2 Planned. On the reference side, 23 Priority items were re-checked: 2 confirmed outright and 21 partially confirmed with corrections, and 14 items had a wrong prevalence count in the first draft. The most consequential corrections: no reference shows a crash-durable approval pause, so durable resume is a Verevon synthesis built on DeepSeek checkpoints; Manus's side-effect taxonomy drives replay deduplication, not approvals; and Claude's hash-chained audit log is a local per-session file, not a hosted compliance store.

## 2. What every reference shares

The comparison found 58 capabilities that are table-stakes across the 12 references: each is present in at least 4 of the sources that could have it. Verevon already matches the references on the chat substrate: streaming, rendering, web search, context compaction, subagents, a typed client contract and identity are shipped. The shared capabilities it lacks or only half has cluster in four places: continuity of the agent loop (run context, plan approval, scheduled runs), the conversation data model (versions, fork, search, projects, sharing), extensibility that actually executes (MCP tools, skill bundles, plugins, connectors) and governance surfaces (model entitlements, policy registry, memory controls, browser site policy). Desktop computer use is the one table-stakes capability that needs a new foundation.

Prevalence is written "n of N references". N is 12 for most categories; where a category could only be assessed on fewer sources the row says so (11 for multimodal and research, 9 for coding). Verevon status comes from the verified recommendation where one exists; where verification corrected a count, the corrected count is used and the row says "corrected".

### 2.1 Chat experience

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Streaming transport and incremental conversation-state sync | 4 of 12 | Shipped | none needed |
| Rich Markdown, math, diagram and safe rendering stack | 6 of 12 | Shipped | none needed |
| Message actions: edit, retry, regenerate with model or effort switch, persisted versions | 4 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012) |
| Branch or fork a conversation from any message, with lineage | 8 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012) |
| Rich composer: typed inline tokens, @-mentions and slash menu | 6 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-052](#F-t3-052) |
| Conversation organisation: pins, stars, archive, unread, sections, snooze, bulk | 9 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012) |
| Conversation search and agent recall of past chats | Disputed; counts conflict across clusters, so no count is stated | Partial | [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012) |
| Projects or workspaces with instructions, knowledge, members and retrieval auto-switch | 6 of 12 (corrected) | Partial | [F-t3-013](#F-t3-013) |
| Sharing: links, snapshots, replay and admin policy | 8 of 12 | Partial (share link absent) | [F-t3-046](#F-t3-046) |

The shared baseline is a durable message tree that users can edit, regenerate, fork and search, organised into projects and shareable by link. Fork from any message is in 8 of 12 references and conversation organisation in 9 of 12, which makes the history model the most widely shared chat capability [SRC/DOC]. Verevon streams and renders at parity, but its history foundation is thin: session-core has thread list, archive and presentation fields but no message parent or sibling, no fork or supersede route, no full-text index and no recall tool (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:137-185; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/migrations/0021_thread_presentation.sql:1-12). Chat projects are a browser-only folder list capped at 100 (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/lib/chat-organization.ts:2-34), while the real project container, Spaces, is not reachable from /chat. Verification notes that persisted versions with a model or effort switch are only 4 of 12 and DOC-only for the model-switch part, so the message tree matters more than the regenerate menu [DOC].

### 2.2 Multimodal & creation

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Multi-format file attachments with published limits and out-of-context processing | 4 of 11 | Partial | [F-t3-017](#F-t3-017) |
| Streaming dictation into the composer | 6 of 11 | Partial (dictation per utterance, no live partials) | [F-t3-082](#F-t3-082) |
| Image generation with model choice and typed results | 4 of 11 | Partial | [F-t3-051](#F-t3-051) |
| Office document creation through skills and a sandbox, with verification | 5 of 11 | Partial | [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014) |
| Sandboxed data analysis on uploaded files | 4 of 11 | Partial | [F-t3-017](#F-t3-017) |

The baseline is a composer that accepts large files of many types, a sandbox that can open those files, and document output that is checked before delivery. Verevon caps attachments at 1 MB, 60,000 characters per file and 120,000 per turn and inlines extracted text into the message, so uploaded files never reach the code interpreter (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/lib/prepare-chat-attachments.ts:6-43). Office files already come out of a bubblewrap sandbox whose image carries openpyxl, python-docx, reportlab and pypdf (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/code_interpreter.rs:1-45), but there is no skill bundle and no render-and-look check. Dictation is ahead of the original assessment: a speech-to-text call followed by a fail-soft cleanup pass is shipped (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/api/audio-client.ts:34-100); only streaming partials are missing. Image generation is a request flag that routes the whole turn to one hard-coded 1024x1024 call, not a tool an agent can use (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/sse.rs:1415-1440). The claude.ai limits conflict between two help articles (500 MB per chat file versus 30 MB in the sandbox article), so no single peer limit should be copied [DOC].

### 2.3 Research & web

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Web search tool with grounded, cited answers | 11 of 11 | Shipped | none needed |
| Connected apps in chat (OAuth connectors with read and write actions, multi-account, @-mention scoping) | 5 of 11 (corrected) | Partial | [F-t3-010](#F-t3-010) |

Cited web search is the only capability every reference has, and Verevon ships it. Connected apps are the gap: the Ingestion side dispatches mail, drive and calendar reads end to end, but execution-core's catalogue omits the single-item and file-content actions and plain chat has no connector reads (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/integration_tools.rs:1-160). Verification dropped Antigravity from the count because its evidence is a generic workspace API step, and notes that the ChatGPT read-versus-write gating is DOC-only and could not be re-fetched [DOC].

### 2.4 Agent runtime

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mid-turn steering and message queueing | 8 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016) |
| Context compaction core (prune, summarise, provider-native) | 7 of 12 | Shipped | none needed |
| Plan mode with reviewable plan and approval gate | 9 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-011](#F-t3-011) |
| Subagent delegation and lifecycle | 7 of 12 | Shipped | none needed |
| Scheduled agent runs (cron or RRULE) with result delivery | 10 of 12 (two vendors double-counted) | Partial | [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018) |
| Layered custom instructions (global, organisation, space, project) applied everywhere | 8 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001) |

The baseline agent loop takes a plan, pauses for approval, accepts steering mid-turn, compacts its own context, delegates to children and runs on a schedule. Verevon has every piece but the joins: approving a plan records a grant and does not start the executing run (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/components/use-chat-controller.ts:2030-2135); queued input is per-process memory (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/queued_input.rs:1-80); each scheduled step is one tool-less 1,024-token completion (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/grpc.rs:498-530); and the agentic branch seeds a fresh history with only the goal, so org, Space and persona instructions reach Ask turns but not Do runs (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:1185-1226). Verification adds two cautions: no reference implements multi-replica run control, so that part of [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016) is Verevon design [INF]; and the scheduled-run count of 10 of 12 counts Manus and Anthropic twice [SRC/DOC].

### 2.5 Tools, plugins & extensibility

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| MCP client runtime: transports, per-server and per-tool config, supervision and recovery | 6 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003) |
| Plugin package: one installable bundle of skills, connectors, commands, agents, hooks and templates | 7 of 12 | Stub | [F-t3-059](#F-t3-059) |
| Skill packages (SKILL.md open standard) with progressive disclosure | 9 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014) |
| Composer slash commands and @ or $ mentions | 9 of 12 | Shipped | none needed |
| Public API, SDKs and programmatic control of the same agent | 10 of 12 | Shipped | none needed |

MCP is the Model Context Protocol, the open standard for connecting tools to a model. The baseline is that MCP tools, skill folders and plugin bundles can all be installed and then actually run. Verevon registers MCP servers and offers their tools to Do runs, but the signed gate returns no capability id for dynamic MCP names and the inline Ask path refuses them by design, so no MCP tool can execute (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/permission/mod.rs:200-293; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/tool_loop.rs:625-642). The plugin registry stores manifest JSON that nothing reads (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/api/plugins.go:13-22). Skills exist as text records with matching and sharing, not as SKILL.md bundles with scripts. Verification found that no reference drives approval policy from a declared side-effect class; Manus declares five levels only for its browser tools and uses them for replay dedupe, so that design in [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003) is a Verevon synthesis [SRC].

### 2.6 Computer & environment control

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Git worktrees for parallel agents | 10 of 12 | Absent (not applicable: not a coding product) | none needed |
| Desktop computer use (screenshots plus accessibility tree, mouse and keyboard on any app) | 8 of 12 | Absent | [F-t3-024](#F-t3-024) |

Computer use is the clearest table-stakes gap: the Do catalogue offers only browser.observe, browser.act and code_interpreter, the execution substrate is a one-shot bubblewrap child, and "desktop" is rejected as a broker grant (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:4190-4222). The owner parked user-desktop control, so the recommendation scopes computer use to the agent's own sandbox desktop and depends on the per-thread sandbox computer in [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009). Git worktrees are IDE table-stakes and are deliberately not adopted.

### 2.7 Coding capability

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Diff review surface with scopes and accept or reject | 5 of 9 | Partial | [F-t3-076](#F-t3-076) |

Verevon renders read-only unified diffs and has an artifact version stepper, and the sandbox lease already computes a changed-file list at release, but nothing shows it to the user and no caller promotes an overlay into the Space (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/workspace_hydrate.rs:102-125). Accept or reject by file or hunk is the nice-to-have step on top.

### 2.8 Memory, learning & personalization

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Account long-term memory with user view, edit, delete, reset and pause controls | 8 of 12 (corrected) | Partial | [F-t3-020](#F-t3-020) |
| First-run onboarding that personalises the agent | 10 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-061](#F-t3-061) |

The baseline is memory the user can see and govern, seeded by an onboarding step that asks who they are. Verevon lists, corrects and deletes memories (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/settings/components/MemorySection.tsx:1-80) and its Dreaming loop already infers user-scope memories every 300 s (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/dreaming.rs:126-215), which puts it ahead of several references on extraction. It lacks an on, paused or off state, a reset-all and any org memory policy. Verification removed Cursor from the memory count and found a distinct pause control only in Claude, so pause is not table-stakes [SRC/DOC]. The onboarding wizard that exists is organisational (website crawl, connect accounts), not personal.

### 2.9 Models & routing

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Plan, role and org model entitlements with admin defaults and caps | 4 of 12 (corrected; common rather than table-stakes) | Absent | [F-t3-023](#F-t3-023) |
| Per-model reasoning-effort ladder mapped to each wire vocabulary | 6 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-029](#F-t3-029) |
| Provider-neutral inference layer: adapters, middleware chain and canonical event stream | 5 of 12 | Shipped | none needed |
| Bring-your-own keys, cloud accounts and customer LLM gateway | 6 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-065](#F-t3-065) |
| Provider prompt-cache management: stability tiers, rolling breakpoints, lineage-scoped cache keys | 5 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-022](#F-t3-022) |
| Frontier vendor breadth: Gemini, native DeepSeek, open-weight and specialist models | 5 of 12 | Absent for Gemini (DeepSeek only as an Azure MaaS id) | [F-t3-041](#F-t3-041) |

Verevon's inference layer is at parity in shape, but thin in reach. The model family enum has only OpenAiCompatible and Anthropic, so no Gemini or Vertex path exists (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/inference-core/src/provider/mod.rs:462-478). Effort is a three-value profile that maps to an Anthropic thinking budget and a Codex reasoning effort, and the OpenAI and Azure builder sends no reasoning effort at all (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/thinking.rs:1-80; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/inference-core/src/provider/openai.rs:430-455). There is no per-org model allowlist because no service owns the OrgPolicy allowed_models field (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/grpc.rs:148-160). Provider registration is environment-only and the only per-user connection is the ChatGPT subscription route (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/inference-core/src/config.rs:200-335). Cache tokens are measured end to end; what is missing is a hit-rate view and the off-by-default stable-prefix order (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/prompt_context.rs:1-60). No reference exposes a hit-rate KPI either, so that part is a Verevon addition [SRC].

### 2.10 Transparency, observability & control

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Inline tool-call cards and work log | 5 of 12 | Shipped | none needed |
| Usage allowance and usage history page | 7 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-028](#F-t3-028) |

Tool-call cards are at parity. Usage is reported to admins through the cost dashboard, but a user cannot see how much of their allowance a run has used: the usage event is emitted once at end of turn with no cache-token fields and there is no running-spend event (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/sse_events.rs:268-286). Verevon's Trace tab and context inspector already exceed most references on what the model saw; the live meter is the missing table-stakes piece.

### 2.11 Safety, permissions & governance

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Named permission modes or presets (ask, accept-edits, plan, auto, full access) | 8 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-032](#F-t3-032) |
| Model-based auto-review of risky actions (approve for me, guardian) | 7 of 12 | Absent | [F-t3-097](#F-t3-097) |
| Agent-browser governance: site allow and block lists, per-site consent, JS policy, origin trust | 7 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008) |
| Prompt-injection defences (untrusted-content labelling, scanning, sanitisation, layered monitoring) | 8 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-033](#F-t3-033) |
| Credential storage and secret references | 7 of 12 | Unknown (no baseline record; connector tokens live server-side) | [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008) |
| Admin agent-policy registry and organisation setting locks | 7 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-032](#F-t3-032) |
| Identity, RBAC, SSO and SCIM and spend limits | 7 of 12 | Shipped | none needed |

The governance baseline is a small set of named modes an admin can lock, site-level browser policy, layered injection defences and an audit of agent actions. Verevon has the primitives: a server-floored approval posture per profile and an autonomy rung on plan approval (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/profile.rs:90-125), trust-class provenance and a fail-closed injection scan (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/moderation.rs:40-110), and a browser-broker grant with allowed domains and actions. What it lacks is the product layer: modes are not a user-facing concept, the injection scan is 16 lowercase substrings, and the gateway still sends a placeholder grant id so no hosted-browser AI run is authorised (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:1195-1275). The model-based reviewer is absent and conflicts with the standing "no LLM on the permission hot path" decision; only a tighten-only design is compatible, which is why [F-t3-097](#F-t3-097) is nice-to-have despite 7 of 12 prevalence. Verification cautions that fail-closed denylists are not the shared practice: Antigravity's denylist fails closed by documentation while Claude's site-category lookup fails open on timeout [DOC/SRC].

### 2.12 UI/UX & product design

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Agent-first app shell and information architecture | 6 of 12 (one vendor double-counted) | Partial | [F-t3-015](#F-t3-015) |
| Attention model: typed notifications, sounds and badges | 5 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-015](#F-t3-015) |
| Theming and appearance customisation | 7 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-098](#F-t3-098) |
| Responsive web, mobile apps and multi-platform reach | 6 of 12 (one vendor double-counted) | Partial | [F-t3-015](#F-t3-015) |
| Internationalisation and localisation discipline | 7 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-066](#F-t3-066) |

The shared shell puts chats and agent runs in one attention-ordered list, tells the user when a run needs them, and reaches them on a phone. Verevon splits chat, the Agent Run Console and Spaces into separate destinations, and its run-watch consumer maps only run-completed and run-failed (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/runwatch/consumer.go:81-100); there is no PWA manifest and no Web Push. Control Plane already persists fontSize and compactMode but the gateway navbar payload drops them (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/navbar.rs:84-104). The most visible i18n debt is Norwegian-only approval buttons and plan labels in the chat (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/components/ChatMessages.tsx:1900-1930). Verification notes that the closed attention set in [F-t3-015](#F-t3-015) is a Verevon design: Claude's kinds are permission, idle and question, and Antigravity distinguishes only action-required from done [SRC].

### 2.13 Platform architecture

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| One agent runtime behind many entry surfaces (desktop, CLI, headless, daemon, SDK, API) | 6 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-064](#F-t3-064) |
| Desktop shell process model (thin native shell plus supervised backend child) | 7 of 12 | Absent (not applicable: web SPA) | none needed |
| Multiple execution environments from one UI (cloud, local, SSH, WSL) | 7 of 12 | Absent (not applicable: cloud-only by design) | none needed |
| One typed RPC contract between every client and the runtime | 6 of 12 | Shipped | none needed |
| Explicit state layout and per-conversation working directory | 6 of 12 | Partial | [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009) |
| Desktop distribution, installers and auto-update | 8 of 12 | Absent (not applicable) | none needed |

Four of the platform table-stakes are desktop or IDE concerns that a cloud web product does not need. The two that apply are one runtime behind many surfaces and a per-conversation workspace. Model Plane already serves the whole run lifecycle over HTTP with an OpenAPI spec and generated SDKs, but only the browser gateway consumes it and no API key is exchanged for a model-gateway bearer (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:132-236). Sandbox-manager has a lease lifecycle and Space-scoped workspace hydration, yet execution runs as a bubblewrap process per call with no persistent per-thread workspace (apps/Model Plane/go/services/sandbox-manager/internal/lease/lease.go:1-20; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/sandbox.rs:270-360). Verification found that the per-task computer count double-counts vendors; roughly five distinct vendors host one, and Firecracker is the only named backend among them [DOC/SRC].

## 3. Best of breed: who does each thing best

The table names the reference whose implementation is the strongest model for each capability area, after the verification corrections. REA means reverse-engineered from the shipped app; SRC, source or shipped files; DOC, public documentation; INF, inference.

| Capability area | Best reference | Why it is best | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Run context seeding | Cursor [SRC] | A typed request context with hydration flags and rule activation modes (always, glob, agent-fetched, manual) confirmed in source [SRC] | [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001) |
| Checkpointed resume | DeepSeek harness [SRC] | Checkpoints before model requests, tool effects and next steps, and cold resume repairs the log tail; crash-durable approval pause is not shown by any reference [SRC] | [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002) |
| MCP client runtime | Hermes [SRC] | Supervised servers with recovery and per-server trust tiers; the readOnlyHint check is binary and per server, not per tool [SRC] | [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003) |
| Per-tool connector policy | Claude desktop [SRC] | Per-tool toolPolicy with code-defined ask defaults and a never-allow set of 8 tools on each built-in connector [SRC] | [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003) |
| Audit and compliance | ChatGPT [DOC], Perplexity [DOC] | Compliance log with a scoped API, and audit JSON streamed by webhook to a SIEM; the ChatGPT page could not be re-verified [DOC] | [F-t3-004](#F-t3-004) |
| Approval decisions | Codex [SRC] with Claude [SRC] | Typed decisions (approved, for session, with amendment, denied, abort) and 55 versioned card templates; Claude adds 33 cause codes and an anti-decoy card [SRC] | [F-t3-006](#F-t3-006) |
| Learning governance | Manus [SRC], Hermes [SRC] | Learning proposals the user accepts or rejects (Manus); opt-in write-approval staging and a skill-mutation ledger with rollback; no reference evaluates learning outcomes [SRC] | [F-t3-007](#F-t3-007) |
| Agent browser vocabulary | Antigravity [SRC], Manus [SRC] | Widest action set with per-action state diffs and a recorded browser subagent; a 22-tool snapshot and ref contract [SRC] | [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008) |
| Browser site governance | Claude [SRC], Perplexity Comet [DOC] | Per-site consent with admin allow and block lists; Browser Control, Read Only or No Access per domain set by org admins [SRC/DOC] | [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008) |
| Per-task computer | claude.ai, Perplexity [DOC]; Cursor [SRC strings] | One isolated machine per task with snapshot and expiry; Firecracker is the only backend named [DOC/SRC] | [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009) |
| Connected apps | Manus [SRC]; Claude desktop [SRC] for policy | Per-task connector toggles and account selection; admin-managed built-in connectors with device-code sign-in and read-only switches [SRC] | [F-t3-010](#F-t3-010) |

| Human takeover | Manus [SRC/DOC], T3 Code [SRC] | Agent-suggested takeover cards with hand-back; real human-versus-agent input arbitration [SRC] | [F-t3-026](#F-t3-026) |
| Deep research | ChatGPT [DOC] | Clarifying questions and an editable plan before the run [DOC] | [F-t3-027](#F-t3-027) |

| Effort ladder | Hermes [SRC] | One canonical ladder clamped per model and mapped per wire format [SRC] | [F-t3-029](#F-t3-029) |
| Artifact canvas | Manus [SRC], claude.ai [DOC] | Server-side versions with restore and editor attribution; AI edits scoped to a selection [SRC/DOC] | [F-t3-031](#F-t3-031) |

| Injection defences | Perplexity [DOC], Claude desktop [SRC] | Most layered documented stack; a homoglyph-aware scrubber that neutralises forged envelope tags [DOC/SRC] | [F-t3-033](#F-t3-033) |

| Gemini capabilities | Antigravity [SRC], Perplexity [DOC] | Gemini thinking levels and typed media parts; the widest model line-up [SRC/DOC] | [F-t3-041](#F-t3-041) |

| MCP Apps and widgets | Codex [SRC] | Full ext-apps implementation with a gated JSON-RPC bridge and form elicitation [SRC] | [F-t3-045](#F-t3-045), [F-t3-044](#F-t3-044) |
| Sharing and replay | Manus [SRC] | Visibility, roles, replay and admin policy on one snapshot model [SRC] | [F-t3-046](#F-t3-046), [F-t3-050](#F-t3-050) |
| Event triggers | Cursor [SRC], Hermes [SRC] | Automations with event triggers, LLM filtering and dry runs; source-hash wake gates and suggestions capped at 5 [SRC] | [F-t3-047](#F-t3-047) |
| Readable long runs | T3 Code [SRC], DeepSeek harness [SRC] | Settled-turn folding; four density levels and a fixed activity vocabulary [SRC] | [F-t3-048](#F-t3-048) |
| Composer | T3 Code [SRC], Antigravity [SRC] | Atomic typed chips with per-thread drafts; typed scope items and server slash commands [SRC] | [F-t3-052](#F-t3-052) |
| Plugins | Claude desktop [SRC], Codex [SRC], Hermes [SRC] | Typed manifest with sha-pinned binaries; open manifest with foreign-namespace adapters; a catalogue pinned to commits with a kill list [SRC] | [F-t3-059](#F-t3-059) |
| Personalisation | Hermes [SRC], Claude desktop [SRC] | Frozen, bounded user snapshot in the prefix; onboarding that writes name, role and topics into instructions [SRC] | [F-t3-061](#F-t3-061) |
| Provider registry and BYO | Hermes [SRC], Claude desktop [SRC] | 41 overridable provider profiles; BYO cloud accounts with helper credentials that expire [SRC] | [F-t3-065](#F-t3-065) |
| i18n and UI gates | DeepSeek harness [SRC], Hermes [SRC] | Typed dictionaries with a gate against hard-coded copy; layered locale packs [SRC] | [F-t3-066](#F-t3-066) |
| Code mode and tool index | Hermes [SRC], Codex [SRC], DeepSeek harness [SRC] | Deferred tool search with core tools never deferred; model-written JavaScript in a V8 isolate; every sub-call re-enters the guarded pipeline [SRC] | [F-t3-070](#F-t3-070) |

No single reference is the model for the whole product. Claude desktop is the best overall model for governed autonomy and calm UX: typed approval contracts, stored permissions for unattended runs, per-site browser consent and document skills with validators, all of which map onto Verevon's signed-gate design [SRC]. Hermes is the model for the Model Plane internals that users never see but feel: prompt-cache stability, the effort ladder, indexed history recall, scheduled runs as full agent runs and governance of what the agent learns [SRC]. DeepSeek harness is the model for transparency and durability: checkpointed resume, the trajectory ledger, typed lifecycle events and plan approval that resumes the run [SRC]. Manus is the model for the computer-using half of the roadmap: sandbox computers, accessibility-first desktop control, takeover, replay, wide research and sharing [SRC/DOC]. T3 Code is the model for the chat shell itself: the inbox sidebar, virtualised timeline, composer chips and idempotent receipts [SRC]. Codex is the model for extensibility contracts: plugin manifests, MCP Apps, typed decisions and plan content [SRC]. Antigravity, Cursor and the web products contribute narrower patterns: the Gemini family and browser subagent, the typed run context and event triggers, and the documented enterprise controls for entitlements, compliance and deep research [SRC/DOC].

## 4. Priority recommendations

Priority items are the gaps that block the agent loop, its governance, or a compliance claim that Verevon already makes. Each one was verified against the CoresSystem code at commit afb83928 (2026-10-10), and the status, effort and approach shown on every card are the verified values, not the first-pass estimates. The cards are listed in recommended build order within the tier; the wave column places each item in the overall programme. Where verification corrected a claim about a reference product, the corrected claim is the one stated here. Prevalence is written as "n of 12 references".

<a id="F-t3-001"></a>
### F-t3-001 · One server-assembled run context for Do, Plan and scheduled runs: instruction stack, thread history, persona, memory and run notes
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Model Plane (model-gateway composer, execution-core loop, session-core assembly) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Give every Do, Plan and scheduled run the same context that an Ask turn already gets: the composed instruction stack (platform, org, Space, persona), the prior turns of the thread under the compaction budget, a memory prefetch and attachments. Add a composed authored-instructions field and bounded prior-turn messages to RunAgentRequest, fill them in model-gateway before the agentic branch returns, and insert them in run_rounds. Stamp completeness flags (instructions, history, memory) on the run record. Automations get a per-schedule notes block later, once [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018) lands.

**Why it matters.** The governed agent loop is the one place where the model takes actions, and today it starts from a preamble plus the goal. Org policy, Space instructions, persona and memory are silently dropped exactly where they matter most. This is a governance gap, not only a quality gap, and it is the cheapest high-impact fix in the audit.

**What the references do.** Cursor renders typed context components to role messages and loads rules from project files, team and user settings with confirmed activation modes: always, glob, agent-fetched and manual [SRC]. DeepSeek chains workspace instruction files under a byte budget [SRC]. Codex passes a typed turn context [SRC]. Claude desktop labels seven instruction scopes (Global, Folder, Space, Project, Channel, Profile, Organization); the only documented precedence is that organization instructions override personal preferences, and the organization budget observed is 3000 characters [SRC, from i18n strings, not runtime code]. Layered instructions are table-stakes at 8 of 12 references; memory in agent tasks is 3 of 12 [SRC/DOC]. The ordering org > Space > persona > user is Verevon's own design, not a reference behaviour [INF]. Caveats: ChatGPT, Claude.ai, Manus and Perplexity instruction layering is DOC-only; Cursor automation memory rests on service names only.

**Verevon today.** Ask turns compose instructions in the ADR-0003 composer (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/sse.rs:6360-6420) and prefetch memory with a 400 ms bound (sse.rs:1655), but the agentic branch returns at sse.rs:1405-1500 before either runs, and build_run_agent_request passes only the content plus an approved-plan block (sse.rs:9118-9190). RunAgentRequest (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/execution.proto:76-200) has no history, instruction, persona or memory field, and run_rounds seeds a fresh preamble-plus-goal history (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:1185-1226). The BFF already injects org, Space and persona text onto the same request on both ingress paths (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/ag_ui.rs:85-130, apps/gateway/src/domains/chat/streams.rs:55-100), so the data reaches model-gateway and is dropped there. Reuse authored_instructions_message and its tests, load_context_assembly_messages, fetch_chat_memory_context, the compaction.rs budget planner and the existing space_id and plan_mode fields. Plan rows B3 and B4 (CHAT_GAP_CLOSURE_PLAN_2026-10-01.md:109-110) cover instructions and hook_context only.

**How to build it in Verevon.** The resolver is not in Model Plane: the BFF resolves (client values stripped, Space layer gated on live Control membership) and model-gateway composes, so execution-core must receive finished text and never re-resolve or read Space data. Hydrate history in model-gateway, which already holds the verified user bearers, rather than giving execution-core a second session-core read path. Subagents keep isolated history but inherit instructions. Memory prefetch must honour Zero Data Retention (ZDR) and the per-thread pause or off switch from [F-t3-020](#F-t3-020). Sequence with [F-t3-022](#F-t3-022) so volatile blocks sit in the append-only tail and the cached prefix stays stable; otherwise larger prompts raise the cost of every run.

**Quick win.** The proto field and gateway composition are S-sized and reuse code that already runs for Ask turns; the gain is immediate for every Do run.

<a id="F-t3-002"></a>
### F-t3-002 · Durable agent loop: checkpoint every round and pause, resume in place after approval, answer or crash, close dangling tool calls, effect ledger
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (execution-core loop and continuation, session-core run state) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Persist the Do loop's message fold in session-core at every round and before each effectful call, and re-enter the loop at that checkpoint when an approval, a clarifying answer or a crash recovery arrives. Stage it: first a continue that starts a fresh run seeded by [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001) context plus completed step summaries (plan B7 and open decision OD6), then true in-place resume from a scrubbed fold checkpoint. Typed closers for interrupted tool calls, an effect ledger, parking on provider exhaustion and an ask_user tool are separable follow-on items.

**Why it matters.** Today an approval is a dead end for a multi-step task: the loop pauses durably but discards its reasoning state, and only three tool kinds are re-executed cold from a descriptor. That blocks plan-continue, scheduled runs with approvals, goals and the computer-use loop.

**What the references do.** DeepSeek checkpoints before each model dispatch, before each tool body and at the next request boundary, fails closed, and resumes with ownership [SRC]. It explicitly disclaims exactly-once: it records dispatch intent, writes an unknown-outcome closer instead of retrying, recommends forwarding the call id as an idempotency key, and does not checkpoint inside an active model attempt; its durable out-of-turn approval workflow is deferred [SRC]. Hermes holds a refreshed turn lease (TTL 300 s) with a stall watchdog, classifies 29 failover reasons, and parks a turn with a visible countdown over five cycles [SRC]; its blocking server-to-client requests use an in-memory event and are replayed only to reconnecting clients while the process lives [SRC]. Durable resume is 5 of 12 references, provider retry and failover 6 of 12 [SRC]. Manus keeps an in-memory action ledger (4096 entries, 10-minute horizon) for browser side-effect actions only, returning a replay-conflict on digest mismatch [SRC]. No reference shows a crash-durable pause and resume after approval; that is a Verevon synthesis [INF]. Cursor auto-continue is server-flag-gated [SRC].

**Verevon today.** pause_for_approval writes an approval with an encrypted descriptor and returns (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:4476-4560); history is a local Vec per run_rounds call (agent.rs:1185-1200). The only continuation is approval_delivery_worker.rs:1-60, which resumes execute_provider_action, book_shipment and tickets.create and fails every other tool closed. resume_durable_approval_continuation always returns unavailable (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/grpc.rs:706-713) and model-gateway never turns a grant into ResumeRun (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/approvals.rs:255-275). Run state is in-memory (execution-core/src/state.rs:1-40). Reuse the session-core checkpoints table and SaveCheckpoint RPC with its 4 MiB cap, the approval outbox and receipt protocol, continuation_crypto.rs, the terminalization recovery loop, stream_buffer replay and approval rehydration, which already exist.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Decide the host explicitly: orchestrator-core has Temporal workflows with approval signals and per-turn activities (apps/Model Plane/go/services/orchestrator-core/cmd/workflows/interactive_run.go:52-55), but they drive ExecuteStep, and the chat Do path bypasses Temporal. ZDR: durable step records are content-redacted under ZDR (agent.rs:4376-4398), so a ZDR run must not checkpoint content durably; refuse resume or keep it memory-only. Resume must re-authorise through Control with a fresh delegated bearer (OD8); a service-principal shortcut would break delegated identity. browser_agent resumption stays excluded until a browser run itself survives a pause, and MCP tools have unknown idempotency, so no exactly-once promise applies to them. Lists [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016) as a dependency, although verification demoted it because approvals already persist in session-core and it is not a precondition for durable runs. Keep the core slice at L; bundling closers, ledger, parking and ask_user makes it XL, so split.

<a id="F-t3-003"></a>
### F-t3-003 · Declared tool side-effect classes, a never-auto set and per-tool allow/ask/never policy, so MCP tools become callable through the signed gate
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (capability-core registry and policy, execution-core and model-gateway enforcement) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Replace name-substring risk guessing with a declared side-effect class on every capability-core row (built-in, provider action, discovered Model Context Protocol (MCP) tool), derived at MCP discovery from tool annotations and pinned by digest. Make trusted_capability_id resolve dynamic mcp__server__tool names through the registry, have the autonomy-rung ladder read the class, seed a non-delegable never-auto set, and store per-tool allow/ask/never policy at user and org scope in capability-core. Deliver Do mode first; read-only MCP in Ask is a later step.

**Why it matters.** The model cannot call any MCP tool today, although registration, OAuth and staged disclosure are built. MCP client runtime is table-stakes at 6 of 12 references. The substring classifier is the weakest link in an otherwise strong signed-gate design: risk should be declared data.

**What the references do.** Hermes runs each MCP server as a supervised task owning one session, with recovery handlers and a circuit breaker; its trust gate is a binary readOnlyHint check applied only to servers configured as untrusted, asked once per call with no stored policy, and the default is full trust [SRC]. Claude desktop has a per-tool toolPolicy for its two built-in connectors (GitHub, Microsoft 365) with code-defined ask defaults, readOnlyHint skipping, a stricter-wins merge, an 8-tool never-allow set per connector and sliding-window write and send throttles [SRC, from prettified bundles]. Manus declares five side-effect levels for its 22 browser tools, but in code they drive only replay deduplication and MCP annotations, not approvals [SRC]. ChatGPT documents graduated app permissions (always ask, read, low-risk, all) [DOC]; Perplexity documents always-ask plus once, thread or deny, with recurring runs inheriting thread grants [DOC]. Using a declared class to drive approval policy is therefore a Verevon synthesis [INF]. The per-tool policy count in the original data was inconsistent and is not restated.

**Verevon today.** execution-core matches name substrings and treats every mcp__ name as risky (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/permission/mod.rs:200-293); capability-core rows carry only a three-level risk_level (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/models/capability.go:1-60) consumed by the policy engine (internal/policy/engine.go:105-155). MCP tools are offered to Do runs, but trusted_capability_id is a static map returning None for them (execution-core/src/capability_policy.rs:736-840), so execute_step_inner refuses every call (runtime_loop/mod.rs:495-565). Ask refuses them by design (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/tool_loop.rs:625-642). McpToolAnnotations::side_effect exists with no production consumer (model-gateway/src/mcp_jsonrpc.rs:55-125); the never-auto set is only tickets.create (permission/mod.rs:72-100). Reuse risk_level, rung_required_for and check_autonomy_rung (permission/mod.rs:100-200), requires_durable_owner_approval, and operation_is_write as the fail-safe model.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Model Plane owns capabilities, so classes and MCP tool rows are registry-owned and bound server-side; a binding may never be synthesised from a model-supplied name (capability_policy.rs:836-838). The code warns that two risk tables diverge (permission/mod.rs:81-92), so the class replaces is_risky_call as the rung input rather than sitting beside it. Hooks are an in-memory registry (model-gateway/src/state.rs:317) and not a policy store. ZDR must propagate: agentic runs carry zdr (execution.proto:100-107), so MCP servers outside the attestation boundary are refused there. Unknown classes default to the strictest; mis-declared classes are silent over-permission, so declarations need review. Ask-mode MCP needs an inline continuation contract (tool_loop.rs:625-638) and would push the item to XL.

<a id="F-t3-004"></a>
### F-t3-004 · Deliver the AI-action audit trail, then make it tamper-evident with a compliance query API and SIEM export
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (session-core outbox) and Control Plane (audit-core, compliance API) | W1 | Medium |

**What to build.** Phase 0 is an operations closure with evidence: deploy the session-core publisher fix and requeue migration from postmortem 0002, canary-replay the 4,992 stuck governed-action events into audit-core, add alert rules on the terminal-row gauge to the monitoring config, and schedule the two other broken audit paths the postmortem lists. Then build a per-org hash chain with retention checkpoints and a verifier inside audit-core's insert path, a scoped compliance read API with time-range export, and a Security Information and Event Management (SIEM) fan-out consumer configured through org-core.

**Why it matters.** Governed agent actions were recorded but never reached the audit store. For a business product that acts on behalf of users this is a live compliance gap, and the Trust Center already presents tool-action aggregates that depend on it.

**What the references do.** The combination of hash chaining, a compliance query API and SIEM export exists in no single reference; it is a Verevon synthesis [INF]. Claude desktop writes a local per-session audit.jsonl in which each entry carries an HMAC chained to the previous hash, with a verifier reporting the first broken entry; the key sits beside the log, so it detects naive edits but not a same-user attacker, and it falls back to unsigned entries when safeStorage is unavailable [SRC]. Perplexity streams audit JSON by webhook to Splunk or Slack, including agent-action events, with no query API or tamper-evidence [DOC]. Claude.ai offers a 180-day enterprise export with actor, IP and device, content excluded and no agent-action events [DOC]. ChatGPT's immutable compliance platform and per-category keys could not be re-verified [DOC, unverified]. Cursor's action audit log is a team-admin setting with the client flag default off [SRC]. About 5 vendors offer some exportable audit trail; all are enterprise-tier.

**Verevon today.** The producer fixes are in the repo (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/audit_publisher.rs:432-480; migrations/0043_audit_outbox_requeue.sql:1-60; commit b9ed9197), but the postmortem (apps/Model Plane/docs/postmortem/0002-session-audit-trail-never-delivered.md:98-117) records them undeployed and the rows unreplayed, deployment cannot be proven from code, and no alert-rule file exists. audit-core has only a per-row sha256 payload_hash for replay idempotency, a hard-delete Purge, and an internal-auth GET /v1/audit capped at 500 rows (apps/Control Plane/audit-core/internal/api/api.go:106-116,197-225; internal/store/store.go:85-140,225-325). Reuse the unique indexes on (source_bus, source_producer, event_id), the service-credential model with Principal, Scopes and Planes, the outbox gauges, and the gateway audit.rs org-scoped proxy.

**How to build it in Verevon.** audit-core and the compliance API are Control Plane; Model Plane only delivers events through its outbox and NATS and never touches audit-core's database. Rows stay content-free (tool name, data category, zdr flag) so ZDR is preserved. Purge hard-deletes after the retention window, so the chain needs a signed head per pruned segment or the verifier fails on legitimate retention; events arrive from several buses, so per-org ordering needs a serialised insert path. Export is a new egress path that must be declared and org-scoped. Depends on the nats-provisioner stream manifest and [F-t3-039](#F-t3-039). Effort rose from M to L because of retention checkpoints and multi-producer ordering.

**Quick win.** Phase 0 is S-sized, needs no new code, and turns a documented false trust claim into a true one.

<a id="F-t3-005"></a>
### F-t3-005 · Unblock Zero Data Retention: attest one provider and run the live proof
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Model Plane (inference-core attestation); Control Plane (org switch) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** An operator and release-management item, not new behaviour. Run the Z-60 live proof on a ZDR-TEST org with nothing attested to prove fail-closed refusals, then complete Z-40 by attesting one ZDR-capable provider surface in inference-core with exact contract artifacts and a review-by date (Azure OpenAI and Azure AI Foundry Claude need separate attestations), deploy the uncommitted waves in the recorded order, and run a second live pass on the attested provider. Land the Z-41b fixes and Z-20 wording before the first attested ZDR answer. Keep waiver minting (Z-15) off.

**Why it matters.** Verevon's ZDR design is the strongest in the comparison: one resolver, a signed non-downgradable claim, evidence-bound attestation, typed localised refusals and fail-closed propagation across planes. With no attested provider every ZDR turn is refused and Temporary chat is unavailable, so a design strength is invisible to customers.

**What the references do.** Claude desktop shows a ZDR toggle for Claude Code with an acknowledgement of terms and a notice that changes may take a few minutes; enabling is gated to qualified accounts, so it is not a self-serve switch [SRC, i18n strings]. Neither Claude nor Claude.ai documents a full matrix of what ZDR disables; observed exclusions are point items (analytics off, sampling grants need ZDR off, the unified experience unavailable to ZDR orgs) [DOC]. ChatGPT's Agents API is US-residency only and not ZDR-eligible, even with a self-hosted sandbox [DOC]. Prevalence is 3 of 12 references, spanning only two vendors, and all enterprise-only. The claim that Verevon's design exceeds them is a design claim, not shipped behaviour [INF].

**Verevon today.** inference-core validates evidence-bound AZURE_OPENAI_ZDR_* and AZURE_ANTHROPIC_ZDR_* fields with digest and review-by lapse checks (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/inference-core/src/provider/zdr.rs:1-160); all default empty (apps/Model Plane/deploy/docker-compose.yml:927-951). The gateway refuses the org switch with 409 zdr_no_attested_provider (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/orgs/settings.rs:120-185). Temporary chat availability already follows the live catalogue (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/DashboardComposer.tsx:1100-1113), so no SPA un-gating is needed; chat-retention.ts is the local-cache purge policy. BUILD_PASS_6_8_2026-10-13.md:13,40,65 lists Z-41b open findings, the deploy order and nothing committed or deployed. Reuse the runbook ZDR_OPERATOR_SEED_RUNBOOK.md, zdr-seed-test-org.mjs and the opt-in Playwright gate spec.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Attesting without real contractual ZDR is a compliance failure, so the attestation must name exact resource and approval artifacts and expire. Deploy order from BUILD_PASS_6_8: session-core, execution-core, model-gateway, inference-core before any waiver minter. Attestation is Model Plane config; the org switch gate lives only in the Frontend gateway and org-core stays ungated by design. Z-20 is held behind Z-14 and Z-41b has open review findings, so wording is not free. Effort rose from S to M for evidence collection, the ordered multi-wave deploy and two live passes.

**Quick win.** The code is built; the blocking step is one attestation plus a proof run, and it unlocks every ZDR org at once.

<a id="F-t3-006"></a>
### F-t3-006 · Approvals v2: scoped decisions, typed cause codes, decision provenance, denial memory and step-up for high-risk effects
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (capability-core, model-gateway approvals, session-core) with Frontend Plane cards | W2 | High |

**What to build.** First carry a typed cause code in Approval.context and render a plain localised reason on the chat card, reusing the browser RiskCategory pattern for tool, hook and capability decisions, with no proto change. Then add decision scope (once, this thread, always-as-a-reviewed-rule held at session scope until confirmed in settings), denial memory within a run, and {actor, verdict, cause, rule_id} in the approval event, run log and audit. Require step-up for never-auto and high-risk effects. Defer a "this run" scope until in-run continuation exists, or build it on the existing autonomy-rung grant.

**Why it matters.** Verevon's approvals are durable and stronger than most references, but the card says only that a tool requires approval and every decision is single-use. That produces approval fatigue and teaches users to click through, which defeats the gate.

**What the references do.** Codex has typed decisions (approved, approved_for_session, approved_with_amendment, approved_mcp_policy_amendment, network-policy allow and deny, denied, abort) and 55 versioned card templates with parameters as labelled fields [SRC]. Claude desktop maps 33 cause-code sentences and has a helper that coerces an "always" rule to session scope; the call site was not traced, so medium confidence [SRC]. Antigravity records an AutoRunDecision as actor (user, system, model, default) by verdict, with a trigger_source on every prompt [SRC]. Manus offers once, this task and always [SRC]; T3 Code has acceptForSession [SRC]; DeepSeek has no scopes and resolves a missing answerer to deny [SRC]. Decision scopes are about 4 of 12 references; cause codes 2 of 12. Step-up bound to an approval is 1 of 12 (Codex, build- or account-gated, from strings only) [SRC]; the ChatGPT passkey record is account security, not approval step-up. The request shape with reason_code and matched_rule is a synthesis [INF].

**Verevon today.** Approvals are durable rows with decided_by and free-text decision_reason (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/orchestration.proto:340-381,738-757), but the decide body is approve or reject plus an optional reason (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/orchestration.rs:455-478) and the chat card renders only Approve and Reject (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/components/ChatMessages.tsx:1885-1945). approval_reason_for returns a generic sentence (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:4440-4500). Typed causes exist only for browser actions (execution-core/src/browser_agent/browser_risk.rs:25-60, localised in BrowserChrome.tsx:168-170). Reuse Approval.context and ApprovalKind, the browser label map, the autonomy-rung grant that plan approval writes, requires_durable_owner_approval as the never-auto seed, and the account-erasure typed confirm plus re-auth (gateway/src/domains/actions/dispatchers.rs:3665-3690) as the step-up precedent.

**How to build it in Verevon.** A "this run" grant only helps once the loop continues after a decision, which is [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002); thread and always scopes do not depend on it, so ship them first. Browser causes are computed in execution-core as a gate on Model-proposed actions while Quarry executes or rejects, so a Quarry rejection cause is passed through, not recomputed. The step-up set depends on the declared classes of [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003); "always" rules land in the policy registry of [F-t3-032](#F-t3-032). Keep never-auto and external-effect approvals single-use, make rules reviewable and revocable, and never let a Space or collaborator grant widen an owner-plane ACL.

<a id="F-t3-007"></a>
### F-t3-007 · Govern and prove the skill-learning loop: learned skills start as owner-scoped proposals, a seeded eval proves the loop, and a learning ledger supports undo
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (session-core learning loop, capability-core skills) with Control Plane (org promotion) and Frontend Plane (inbox, ledger) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** The cheap safe fix first: pass the run's user id through SessionRef, add scope, owner_user_id and a disabled-by-default status to UpsertAgentSkillRequest, write learned skills as user-scoped proposals, stop a later upsert from re-enabling an admin-disabled row, and return origin in the capability-core list so the Skills page can label learned skills. Fix the gateway skill cache for per-user visibility. Then a Settings > Learning inbox (diff, reason, source run) with org promotion as "admin copies a proposal into an org skill", a seeded end-to-end eval with cost controls, and a skill_revisions table projected with memory lineage into a "What Verevon learned" ledger with undo.

**Why it matters.** The loop runs by default and its candidates become org-wide matchable skills with no owner, review or status. One user's run can change every colleague's agent, learned skills are an injection persistence vector, and the loop has never been shown to produce a useful skill.

**What the references do.** Manus records each learning proposal as a KnowledgeEvent with PENDING, ACCEPTED or REJECTED status linked to the session event that produced it, surfaced as suggested knowledge the user accepts or rejects [SRC]. Hermes forks a background review after the reply is delivered, only if a final response exists and the turn was not interrupted; it runs on the main model by default to reuse the warm prompt cache (a cheaper model is optional), is cadence-triggered every 10 user turns, is capped at 16 iterations with a whitelisted tool set and read-before-write, and is skipped for cron [SRC]. Hermes stages unattended memory replace and remove fail-closed, but its skill write-approval gate is opt-in (default off) [SRC]. Its skill ledger records a coarse actor (curator, agent, user) with before and after manifests, capped at 5 MB, and describes itself as telemetry, not a gate; it has no run id and does not cover memory [SRC]. Proposal flows are 4 of 12 references; a learning ledger is Hermes only. No reference evaluates learning outcomes; the seeded eval is Verevon's own addition [INF]. Claude's proposal UI is inferred from module names [INF, low].

**Verevon today.** The loop is on unless LEARNING_REVIEW_ENABLED is set (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/cmd/main.go:743-800) and persists through UpsertAgentSkill, which carries only origin (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/sessions.proto:842-900; session-core/src/grpc.rs:5075-5148), so DB defaults make every learned skill scope org, enabled and matchable by the whole org. agent_skills has no status column or revision table; the capability-core list omits origin (internal/api/registry_apis.go:100-260); SessionRef drops the envelope's user_id (go/services/capability-core/internal/sessionreview/trigger.go:40-44; the envelope carries it at go/pkg/envelope/envelope.go:30-41); SkillPromotionWorkflow persists nothing (orchestrator-core/cmd/workflows/skill_promotion.go:1-60); plan I23 is deferred. Reuse the scope, owner_user_id and shared_with columns (migrations/0031_agent_skills_ownership.sql), the gateway OwnershipStore, agent_memory review_state and 0037 lineage, and the existing ZDR and waived-run withholding in learning_events.rs and sessionreview/retention.go.

**How to build it in Verevon.** MatchSkills and fetch_skill_context pull ListAgentSkills once per org per TTL with the triggering caller's bearer and prune anything not returned, so user-scoped skills would flap unless the cache is pulled with a service-wide view or keyed per user [INF from both paths]. Skills support only org or user scope (CHECK constraint), so Space-audience runs are excluded or held as proposals. Do not revive PromoteSkill; promotion goes through the admin-only org-skill creation path. ZDR and waived runs stay excluded. The review call uses a service credential (OD4 open). Screening from [F-t3-033](#F-t3-033) applies; depends on [F-t3-020](#F-t3-020). The staging-gate slice is about M and ships first.

<a id="F-t3-008"></a>
### F-t3-008 · Agent browser in chat and Do mode on real broker grants, with per-site read/act consent, admin allow/block lists and model-blind logins
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | XL (split into grant plus Do tool, L; site governance, L) | Model Plane (grant minting, browser subagent) with Ingestion Plane (Quarry execution), Control Plane (site policy) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Land the planned rows E1, E2, E4, E9, E10 and E12: the gateway mints a real browser-broker grant per run under the user's delegated bearer, the broker owns the domain fence, and a browser-subagent step opens the Quarry run and lease so the model's browser.observe and browser.act calls carry a grant. Then the net-new governance: a per-user site table (control, read only, no access) intersected with Control-owned admin allow and block lists and the Space decision, with block winning; per-action state diffs; a read-only browsing objective usable from Ask. Model-blind logins start with persistent profiles plus human takeover (E10); a vault with handle substitution in Quarry is a separate later item.

**Why it matters.** Verevon already has the strongest browser contract in the comparison (revision-bound refs, risk-classified approvals, Model proposes and Quarry executes), but it is unreachable from chat. A working, governed agent browser is the first visible step toward the "acts like a PC" vision, and site governance is table-stakes at 7 of 12 references.

**What the references do.** Antigravity separates page access from action permission (PAGE_ACCESS and ACTION_PERMISSION waiting reasons, execute_url actuation grants), keeps per-run lists of denied and un-allowlisted sites, and runs a Google-maintained denylist that fails closed and wins over the local allowlist [SRC; fail-closed is DOC]. Manus exposes 22 browser tools bound to a document revision, and its text input refuses on secret origin mismatch [SRC]. Claude desktop ships an admin default site policy with allowed and blocked domains, localhost never blocked, a per-site "asked before each action" prompt and an allow-all switch; its site-category lookup fails open on timeout [SRC]. Perplexity Comet lets enterprise admins set Browser Control, Read Only or No Access per domain [DOC]. Hermes' vault fill re-asserts the origin inside the injected script [SRC]. A full agent browser toolset is at least 6 of 12 references including Claude desktop; model-blind credentials 4 of 12. The six-step consent pipeline is a Verevon synthesis [INF]. Manus live view and take-over are DOC-only.

**Verevon today.** The BFF sends grant_id "session:{id}" (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:1195-1275) while model-gateway validates with the broker and fails closed (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/browser_run.rs:138-200); AcquireGrant appears only in a test double (browser_run.rs:808-826). The Do loop advertises browser.observe and browser.act (execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:4204-4212) but dispatches without a grant (runtime_loop/mod.rs:395-460), so every call fails; inline chat refuses browser_agent (tool_loop.rs:630-646). The broker grant store is an in-memory map (apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/internal/grant/grant.go:62-69). Reuse AcquireGrant and ValidateGrant, resolve_browser_grant, ValidatedBrowserGrant with its risk classes and durable approvals, persistent profiles with the persistent_cookie_use gate, and Quarry's navigation, SSRF and network guards.

**How to build it in Verevon.** browser.act accepts only snapshot, generation and ref, so Quarry stays the final enforcer of every step. Grant authority belongs to the broker and is minted under the BrowserBroker bearer, never from request JSON; domain policy ownership is OD13 and admin lists belong to Control Plane, with Space decisions only narrowing. Grants vanish on broker restart, which matters for long runs. Browser runs are already refused under ZDR; logged-in profiles carry personal data and need retention rules. Page text stays untrusted. Depends on [F-t3-006](#F-t3-006) for per-site consent cards.

<a id="F-t3-009"></a>
### F-t3-009 · Per-thread sandbox computer behind a SandboxBackend seam: microVM backend, persistent thread workspace, typed executor protocol and visible lifecycle
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | XL | Model Plane (sandbox-manager, execution-core) with Control Plane (lease decisions) and cost-core metering | W2 | Medium |

**What to build.** Put a backend abstraction on execution-core's spawn/execute contract (a Rust trait with bubblewrap as implementation 1 and a remote-executor client as implementation 2), and keep sandbox-manager as the lease and registry authority, extended with typed lifecycle, failure-cause and heartbeat fields. Extend the content-addressed (CAS) workspace from Space scope to thread scope so non-Space chats keep state between turns. Land a microVM (micro virtual machine) backend later as a second attested executor plus sandbox-manager pair with its own backend_id, stopped when idle and snapshotted to encrypted object storage, with VM-seconds metering. Show lifecycle state, failure cause and liveness in the chat UI.

**Why it matters.** Five reference vendors (Cursor, Manus, Anthropic, Perplexity, OpenAI) give each task its own hosted computer. Verevon runs hermetic per-call processes that cannot install tools, keep state or host a browser. This item is the foundation for file analysis at scale, app building and the computer panel, and the largest single architectural gap against the Manus and Claude vision.

**What the references do.** Five distinct vendors (Cursor, Manus, Anthropic, Perplexity, OpenAI) give a task a hosted per-task computer; earlier counts double-counted vendors, so no n-of-N figure is stated here. Cursor's agent system prompt describes a Docker container inside a Firecracker VM with a shared tmux session the user can watch, and a client proto enum names Firecracker versions; the orchestration itself is server-side and not inspectable [SRC]. Manus ships a typed executor protocol (HOST_KIND_OPERATOR / SANDBOX_RUNTIME) in binary strings and protobuf names; the direction (cloud proposes, host executes) is inferred from message names [SRC][INF]. Perplexity's per-task microVM and claude.ai's per-conversation VM with snapshot and 30-day expiry are documentation only [DOC]. Claude desktop has a VM startup state machine with typed failure causes and a 30-second heartbeat with a five-strike restart, but it governs a local VM on the user's PC and the heartbeat is flag-gated; it is a transferable liveness pattern, not a hosted microVM [SRC]. Hermes' seven environment backends sit behind a spawn-per-call shell contract, not VM lifecycles [SRC]. Warm pools, vsock guest agents and Kata or gVisor are Verevon design, not reference evidence [INF].

**Verevon today.** sandbox-manager is a Postgres lease, snapshot and process-registry service with a SCRATCH/ACTIVE/SNAPSHOTTING/DESTROYED enum and Control-signed decisions pinning a lease to one backend_id (apps/Model Plane/go/services/sandbox-manager/internal/lease/lease.go:1-20,150-160; apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/sandboxes.proto:11-60). execution-core runs bwrap per call plus an opt-in background-process host (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/sandbox.rs:270-360; process_host.rs:1-25). The Space-scoped CAS workspace is wired in sandbox_lease.rs:1-50,431-510, but the inline ExecuteStep chat path still loses edits (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/chat/shared.rs:205-245). The decision ledger declined a SandboxBackend trait until a second backend exists (apps/Model Plane/docs/decisions/ledger.md:1358-1375). Reuse the SandboxCapabilityProfile, the lease sweeper, workspace_cas.rs and workspace_hydrate.rs, and the nats-provisioner contract-test approach for backend conformance tests.
