**How to build it in Verevon.** Write the ADR that reverses the 2026-08-22 deferral first. Smallest slice: thread-scoped lease and workspace on bwrap, which also fixes the inline-ExecuteStep loss. Then the execution-core trait and typed lifecycle, then the microVM pair gated by the existing Control decision. Constraints: a thread workspace needs its own Control-signed decision and must not alias Space workspaces (Space access never widens); Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) runs stay ephemeral with no snapshot and no content in logs; any browser or desktop inside a VM still goes through Quarry-brokered grants (Model proposes, Quarry executes). Depends on [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002) and feeds [F-t3-024](#F-t3-024). Risks: idle-VM and snapshot cost, kernel patching and supply chain become Verevon's responsibility.

<a id="F-t3-010"></a>
### F-t3-010 · Connected apps that work in chat: prove Gmail, Outlook, Drive and Calendar reads end to end, add Ask-mode reads, @-connector scoping, citations, then approved writes
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (tools, scoping, citations) with Ingestion Plane (integration-corev2 provider actions) and Control Plane (connection ownership) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Refresh execution-core's provider-action catalogue to the operations integration-corev2 really implements, ship a read-only provider tool that does not stall on approval, and reuse that same tool in Ask mode. Add an @connector mention with per-turn account scoping and connector citation chips, prove reads against live mailboxes, then enable writes behind per-action approval.

**Why it matters.** Connected apps are how a business assistant becomes useful on the user's own data. The plumbing exists on both sides but has never run end to end against a real account, and users cannot ask about their own mail in normal chat.

**What the references do.** Connected apps appear in 5 of 11 references (Antigravity's generic WorkspaceAPI step is name-based inference and is dropped from the count) [INF]. ChatGPT connectors are the breadth reference: a connector registry with per-connector action catalogues tagged read or write, writes gated by confirmation and an @mention that narrows the turn to one connector; this rests on help pages only and the page returned HTTP 403 on re-check [DOC]. Manus confirms per-session connector toggles, per-session account selection and project-enabled connectors in shipped code [SRC]; its explicit > project > user-default resolution order is in its public API docs [DOC]. Claude desktop bundles a GitHub server with device-code sign-in, a token cache keyed by host, client and scope, read-only and toolset switches, and admin policy entries; this applies only to admin-managed built-in connectors and is version-gated, so treat it as the enterprise-policy reference [SRC]. Hermes runs an in-chat connect flow as a typed state machine (pending, initiated, connected, skipped, failed, expired) through a hosted, entitlement-gated gateway [SRC].

**Verevon today.** integration-corev2 dispatches mail.messages and .get, gmail.messages and .get, drive.files, drive.file.content and windowed calendar reads (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/actions/service.go:195-310; mail_read.go:1-70), and the composer picker attaches extracted text from those providers (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/DashboardComposer.tsx:150-200). execution-core's catalogue still says no search filter and omits the .get and content operations, so operation_is_write returns None and the calls are approval-gated (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/integration_tools.rs:1-160,744-761; permission/mod.rs:168-225). The inline Ask loop offers only Verevon-native reads (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/tool_loop.rs:3580-3700). mail_read.go records that live provider behaviour is unverified. Reuse IntegrationActionsClient, the is_risky_call classification, the record_tool_step redaction path and plan rows F2-F13 and G7-G10.

**How to build it in Verevon.** First slice: catalogue refresh plus the read-only tool (plan F6). Land F8 connector redaction and untrusted-content framing before enabling it on non-ZDR runs, because record_tool_step persists truncated tool output. Add the new tool to the org class in tool_loop.rs ORG_CLASS_TOOLS so a web-only turn does not offer it (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/source_validation.rs:11-23). Tokens stay in integration-corev2: the model proposes, integration-corev2 executes. Personal mailbox content must never surface in a shared-Space thread, and the org-wide inbox tools are not the F6 target (OD11). Writes stay out of Ask mode, which has no approval gate. Depends on [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003) and [F-t3-006](#F-t3-006), owner decisions OD10/OD12 and Google/Microsoft OAuth app verification. Risks: prompt injection through mail bodies and token lifetime on long runs.

<a id="F-t3-011"></a>
### F-t3-011 · Finish human-in-the-loop: plan approve-then-continue, a typed ask_user tool, and docked approval, plan, question and queue trays
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (tool, pause state, events) with Frontend Plane (trays, cards) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Make plan approval start the executing run, with the grant persisted in session-core. Add ask_user as a non-effectful builtin that pauses the run through the existing durable approval pipeline, emits a user_question event and renders a card with single or multi select, free text and skip. Dock pending approvals, plan proposals, questions and the message queue as trays above the composer in priority order.

**Why it matters.** Plan mode with an approval gate is in 9 of 12 references and structured clarifying questions in 6 of 12. Verevon has the editable plan card but the run stops after approval, and an agent cannot ask a question, so it guesses or stalls.

**What the references do.** Codex is the model for plan content: a decision-complete proposed_plan after a read-only interview, and its request_user_input tool offers optional questions only and never asks for permission [SRC]. Codex is not a model for approve-then-continue: its Plan Mode tells the model not to ask whether to proceed and the user starts implementation in a new turn, which is what Verevon does today [SRC]. DeepSeek's plan review is an ask_user wait with an Approve card, so approval resumes the same run; its question card has a countdown, drafts and late replies that arrive as user messages, and timed mode is opt-in [SRC][DOC]. Antigravity's AskQuestion proto carries options, multi-select, write-in and skip, with PLANNING/EXECUTION/VERIFICATION modes [SRC]. Claude desktop's ask_user_question adds drag-to-rank, evidenced by analytics events and strings only [SRC]. Manus structured questions with single or multiple selection are public API documentation; its shipped code shows typed approval and confirmation messages only [DOC][SRC]. Cursor docks plan, questionnaire, queue and goal trays above the prompt in its new agent window, flag-gated [SRC].

**Verevon today.** approveTurnPlan records the grant and turns plan mode off but starts nothing, and warns the next message may lack the grant (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/components/use-chat-controller.ts:2030-2135). The grant lives in the gateway's in-memory PlanModeStore (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/coordinator.rs:40-215). No ask_user tool, event or card exists in Model Plane or Frontend. The durable approval pause exists: execution-core mints an approval, flips the run to AwaitingApproval and resume re-invokes run_agent (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:4441-4540). Approval, plan and queue cards render inside the transcript (ChatMessages.tsx:1889,2105,2217). Reuse session-core SetRunMode, PlanApprovalControl, ApprovalRequests, QueuedInputStrip and the composer dock in ChatPage.tsx:880-960.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Sequence plan continuation first (plan rows B2, B5, B7): move the grant into session-core and start the run on approval, minting and validating the grant server-side and binding it to run receipts. Model ask_user as a new interaction kind in the existing pause, not a second state machine, and budget it per run. ZDR constraint: zdr_posture::continuation_gate refuses plan approval and resume for ZDR credentials (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/zdr_posture.rs:247-260), so ZDR threads need an in-stream, non-durable ask or none. The trays are a ChatPage layout change and can ship alone. Depends on [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002), [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001), [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016) and the attention events of [F-t3-015](#F-t3-015). Risk: over-asking degrades the experience.

<a id="F-t3-012"></a>
### F-t3-012 · Conversation history foundation in session-core: durable message tree with versions and fork, full-text search, a recall tool, paging and thread organisation
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (session-core single writer, model-gateway routes) with Frontend Plane (sidebar, versions UI) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Ship cursor paging that removes the 80-thread cap, an owner-scoped Postgres full-text index with a search route and sidebar search, and a read-only recall_conversations tool whose snippets are treated as untrusted data. Then fork-as-copy with lineage, and last a persisted message tree (parent pointer, sibling ordinal, active leaf) so regenerate and edit become versions and rewind is a confirmed supersede, never a delete. Add unread and star fields and wire the existing server-side pin and archive.

**Why it matters.** Users notice at once when regenerated answers vanish on reload or old chats cannot be found. Fork is in 8 of 12 references and thread organisation in 9 of 12. Persisted versions with a model or effort switch are 4 of 12, with the model or effort part documentation only.

**What the references do.** Hermes is the only verified indexed-search reference: an FTS5 plus trigram index with BM25 discovery, and one session-search tool with discover, scroll-around-an-anchor, read and browse shapes, no model calls, a 300-row scan limit and lineage dedupe [SRC]. Hermes rewind is a confirmed truncation that archives the cut turns, with durable row ids as authority and no version tree [SRC][DOC]. Claude desktop search is a worker-backed substring scan over local JSONL transcripts capped at 200 recent sessions, not an index, and its search_session_transcripts tool description treats snippets as data, not instructions; fork is forkSession(parentSessionId, forkAtMessageUuid) with lineage strings; both are Claude Code Desktop features, not claude.ai [SRC]. DeepSeek's @label session reference pins a bounded read-only snapshot as durable untrusted context [SRC]. T3 Code's thread menu covers pin, snooze, mark-unread, archive and regenerate-title [SRC]. The 1-of-N version tree with a model or effort override comes from ChatGPT and Perplexity help pages, which returned HTTP 403 on re-check [DOC].

**Verevon today.** The gateway has list, archive, presentation, messages and replay routes but no fork, rewind, supersede or search (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:137-185). No tsvector or trigram index exists in session-core migrations. recall_memory is semantic memory and reattach_context reads only the current thread (tool_loop.rs:3142,4294-4302). The BFF list is capped at MAX_THREADS = 80 (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/chat/history.rs:21,340-365). Versions are client-only and session-lifetime because an earlier persisted design was rejected in review (apps/Model Plane/docs/CHAT_RESUME_AND_VERSIONS_SPEC.md:1-80; chat-versions.ts:1-60). Reuse threads.pinned_at/archived_at (migration 0021), the agent_memory supersedes_id/is_latest transaction (migration 0037) and session-core ListConversation.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Decouple the pieces: paging, index, search route and recall tool first (M, independent of branch semantics), then fork (plan I8), then supersede (I9) only after owner decision OD19 and after ListConversation returns the active path for every consumer (context assembly, learning review, Dreaming, compaction, audit). The index lives in session-core, never in Data Plane v2 (no cross-plane DB). Exclude ZDR turns from the index and honour the durable erase on thread delete. Space-audience threads need a Control-signed Space read decision for search and recall, and recall stays owner-scoped as a prompt-injection channel. Depends on [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016). Risk: one migration for four features; sequence the tree last.

<a id="F-t3-013"></a>
### F-t3-013 · Make Spaces the chat project: instructions, knowledge, members and a retrieval indicator in Ask and Do
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Application Plane (membership, projections) with Model Plane (instruction and scope layering) and Data Plane v2 (knowledge) | W2 | Medium |

**What to build.** Resolve owner decision OD19 between server-synced chat folders with a project-instruction layer (plan I21) and private-room Space projects, then replace the browser-only organiser with the chosen container and add a project picker to the chat sidebar. Independently, layer Space-authored instructions into Do runs, and add a knowledge-mode badge (in context versus retrieval) to the chat.

**Why it matters.** Projects are how comparable products organise ongoing work, and for Verevon they carry org governance: who sees what and which knowledge a run may retrieve. Today chat projects do not survive a browser change and Do runs ignore Space instructions, so project-scoped agents are visibly missing.

**What the references do.** Projects with instructions, knowledge and members appear in 6 of 12 references, which are only four vendors (Anthropic, Manus, OpenAI, Perplexity); DeepSeek's workspace registry groups sessions by directory without instructions or members and does not count [SRC][DOC]. Manus is the best mechanism: embedded protobuf descriptors show a ProjectService with 37 methods (six are sidebar and pin or reorder operations), member roles READ/WRITE/FULL_ACCESS, shared-task policies and personal instructions; server behaviour was not observed [SRC]. ChatGPT projects hold chats, files, instructions and sources [DOC]. The switch from in-context knowledge to retrieval with a visible indicator is Anthropic-only: Claude desktop ships UI strings such as "Searched project knowledge" and claude.ai documents the automatic switch [SRC][DOC]. Multi-user use (Manus up to 49 collaborators, Perplexity head-checked commits) is documentation only [DOC].

**Verevon today.** The sidebar organiser is a localStorage folder list with a 100-project cap (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/lib/chat-organization.ts:2-34), and /chat deliberately has no Space entry point (use-chat-controller.ts:2274-2286). Space-as-project already exists on the Space page with instructions, a knowledge panel, members and a multi-author room timeline with author attribution, reply and @agent mention (SpacePage.tsx:434,626-720; SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:60-100). The functional gap is that authored instructions are composed only on the inline path (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/sse.rs:1855, after the agentic return at sse.rs:1443-1520) and RunAgentRequest has no authored_instructions field (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/execution.proto:76-170). retrieval_notice means a reduced answer, not a knowledge mode (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/api/chat-client.ts:246-250). Reuse inject_authored_instructions plus resolve_space_role (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:4281-4330), authored_instructions_message (sse.rs:6375-6420) and RunAgentRequest.space_id.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Split out Do-run instruction layering as its own small piece (plan B3): one proto field, composed in the agentic branch, riding the request and never logged (ZDR). Render the badge by extending the source_scope_applied event (chat-client.ts:556-566), not retrieval_notice. Any Space picker goes through resolve_space_role and the Control-signed Space decision, never a client-supplied space_ref; Space access never widens an owner ACL. Note that making Spaces the chat project reverses a deliberate design that keeps Space conversations in the Space timeline. Depends on [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001), [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012) and the OD19 ruling. Risks: authority confusion over whose delegated token runs a collaborator-triggered turn; keep collaboration a later phase.

<a id="F-t3-014"></a>
### F-t3-014 · Verified Office deliverables through executable SKILL.md bundles: docx, xlsx, pptx and pdf skills with structural checks, render-and-look and an in-panel previewer
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | XL | Model Plane (capability-core skills, execution-core sandbox, model-gateway) with Frontend Plane previewer | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Three deliverables with one owner each: (a) a first-party verify-and-render step inside code_interpreter, where the agent runs a checker (zip and XML validity, relationships, formula recalculation, slide count, page thumbnails through headless LibreOffice) before returning a typed artifact; (b) SKILL.md bundles (instructions plus references, templates and scripts) stored in the content-addressed store with a skill_file read tool and read-only mounting in the sandbox; (c) a renderer registry in the artifact panel that previews docx, xlsx and pptx.

**Why it matters.** Office files are the main deliverable of an assistant that does work, and today they reach users without any check, so broken files reach users. Office creation through skills and a sandbox is in 5 of 11 references (four vendors) and SKILL.md packages in 9 of 12; verify-and-render and previewers are in 2 of 11 each.

**What the references do.** Claude desktop is the best verify-and-render reference: it bundles six always-on skills, of which four are document skills with scripts and three (docx, pptx, xlsx) share an OOXML schema validator and a LibreOffice conversion helper [SRC]. Its docx skill converts to PDF, rasterises pages and reads the images; pptx renders a thumbnail grid and validates after each write; xlsx makes a recalculation script mandatory with zero formula errors [SRC]. Runtime execution was not observed. claude.ai documents the open skill format with three-stage loading; the 200-character description limit is the claude.ai upload UI, not the standard [DOC]. DeepSeek ships docx, pptx and xlsx skills only, with a checker that explicitly does not judge pagination, clipping, fonts or Excel recalculation; rendering comes from a separate LibreOffice Kit [SRC][DOC]. DeepSeek's document previewer is the best previewer reference: register by extension, 16 MiB and 250,000-cell Excel caps, Office-to-PDF with a missing-font notice [DOC]. Manus has filePreviewer and officePreviewer slices, evidenced by strings only [SRC]. Hermes caps skill prompt descriptions at 60 characters [SRC].

**Verevon today.** Office files already come from code_interpreter, a bubblewrap sandbox with no network whose image carries openpyxl, xlsxwriter, python-docx, reportlab and pypdf, and whose tool description steers to native charts and live formulas (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/tool_loop.rs:4310-4325; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/code_interpreter.rs:1-45; Dockerfile:55-80). Skills are markdown text in an in-memory registry (model-gateway/src/skills.rs:1-30); no bundle code, LibreOffice or pdftoppm exists in Model Plane. The artifact panel previews PDF inline and shows other binaries as a download chip (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/components/ChatArtifactPanel.tsx:442-455,555-570). Reuse workspace_cas.rs (the first S3 content-addressed client), capability-core agent_skills CRUD, and plan tasks H5, H6, G6 and H8.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Start with (a), which needs only a LibreOffice and fonts image layer (owner ruling OD18) and no bundle format. Keep the hermetic guarantees: files_in accepts flat names only, so a read-only bundle mount is a new staging path that must preserve no-network and per-call workspace. Tenant bundle scripts run only inside the sandbox bound to the low-risk cap.command.sandbox, without widening that binding, and third-party bundles follow the supply-chain rules of [F-t3-059](#F-t3-059). Bundle content and thumbnails follow the run's ZDR retention class [INF]. Depends on [F-t3-017](#F-t3-017). Risks: image size and cold start, LibreOffice parser CVEs, fidelity gaps against Microsoft Office. Split as above the effort is roughly L + M + M.

<a id="F-t3-015"></a>
### F-t3-015 · Attention model for long-running agents: closed run-state vocabulary, attention-ordered work list, typed alerts with Web Push, a live shell stream and an installable PWA
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | XL | Model Plane (run-state, shell stream) with Application Plane (notification-core) and Frontend Plane (sidebar, PWA) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Publish one server-derived run-state enum (working, needs_approval, needs_input, plan_ready, completed, failed, archived) on thread rows and rebuild the sidebar around it (Waiting for you, Working, Unseen results, Settled), mixing chats and agent runs. Emit typed attention events as notification-core workflow types with a per-user channel matrix, add a tab badge, Web Push and an installable progressive web app (PWA), and replace REST refetch with a per-user shell stream.

**Why it matters.** Agents that run for minutes or hours are only useful if users learn when they are needed. Verevon has a per-run "notify me when done" watcher but free-form status strings mapped to two chip tones and no push.

**What the references do.** Typed notifications are in 5 of 12 references; the multi-platform and mixed-Recents counts include same-vendor pairs, so they are about one vendor lower than the recorded 6 of 12. Claude desktop has per-type off, badge or banner settings for permission, idle and question, system or no sound, dock bounce, and a sessions-waiting-for-you list; sidebar sections, group-by and auto-archive are evidenced by tool names and strings only [SRC]. T3 Code is the inbox reference: pinned, active with status pills and elapsed time, snoozed, and a collapsed Settled tail [SRC]. Its server attaches the live event buffer before taking the snapshot, coalesces per thread per window, and replays after the client's sequence marker [SRC]. Antigravity plays distinct sounds for action-required and done; its web push is a single RegisterWebPush RPC with an FCM token in the cloud API proto, and PWA support is an app-variant enum plus a detection branch, not observed as shipped [SRC]. The closed set needs_approval, needs_input, finished, failed is a Verevon synthesis, not a reference practice [INF]. claude.ai and ChatGPT mobile parity and push are documentation only [DOC].

**Verevon today.** The watcher runs end to end: RunNotifyButton.tsx to capability-core runwatch to notification-core types run_completed and run_failed, but runwatch maps only those two (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/runwatch/consumer.go:81-100; apps/Application Plane/notification-core/migrations/013_register_run_watch_notifications.up.sql:1-14). Run state travels as free strings (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/runs.proto:282; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/core/components/CoreSidebar.tsx:869-900; chat-organization.ts:47-62). No PushManager, VAPID or manifest code exists in verevonv3 or Application Plane. Typed seeds exist: execution-core's RunStatus enum (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/state.rs:16-40) and run.paused_for_approval event names whose orchestrator-core publishers the broker drops. Reuse notification-core preferences and retention_mode standard|zdr, the CoreNavbar unread badge and the per-run run_events SSE.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Model Plane publishes the enum and typed events; Application Plane owns push subscriptions and the per-user stream; Frontend owns the PWA and sidebar. Ship three deliverables: (1) enum plus needs_approval, needs_input and plan_ready events plus sidebar ordering (L alone), (2) Web Push plus manifest, (3) shell stream. Every notification is content-free (generic title plus deep link) on all threads, because notification-core's README says the full payload still reaches Novu and ZDR delivery is unproven. Typed run-state events must come from session-core's outbox, or the orchestrator-core publish grants must be fixed first (apps/Model Plane/go/services/nats-provisioner/publishers_registry_test.go:70-73; apps/Model Plane/docs/postmortem/0002-session-audit-trail-never-delivered.md:260-278). The service worker never caches authenticated content. Depends on [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016) and [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012). Risk: notification noise.

<a id="F-t3-017"></a>
### F-t3-017 · Attachments v2: fail closed on unsupported media, durable reference-based uploads, and sandbox analysis of the user's files
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (gateway, execution-core) with Data Plane (blob store, extraction) and Frontend Plane (composer, queue) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Phase 1 is client-side: read the vision feature flag from the model catalogue, disable or explain image attach on routes without it, and send images as parts on vision routes instead of diverting the whole turn to AnalyzeImage. Phase 2 adds a bounded attachment-reference field to RunAgentRequest, resolved by model-gateway from an ephemeral per-run store, so attachments reach the code_interpreter workspace read-only (plan H6). Durable reference-based uploads, PDF page images and OCR (optical character recognition) are a later phase sized against owner ruling OD18 (4 MB, 60,000 characters).

**Why it matters.** Uploading a spreadsheet and asking for analysis is expected of any assistant (4 of 11 references for attachments, three vendors). Dropping an image without telling the user contradicts Verevon's own "never fake success" rule.

**What the references do.** Fail-closed handling is confirmed in two sources: DeepSeek's read_image refuses when the model capability is unknown before any file work, and Antigravity's model config carries supported_mime_types per model with the copy "Selected model does not support this media type" [SRC]. claude.ai documents 500 MB per chat file and 20 files, PDFs up to 100 pages visually, 30 MB project files, and 30 MB per file inside the file-creation sandbox; the limits differ by surface [DOC]. Its sandbox egress tiers (off, package managers, package managers plus domains, all) are confirmed for Team and Enterprise with plan-specific defaults [DOC]. DeepSeek's attachment store is content-addressed by sha256 with dedup, on a single machine [SRC]. T3 Code queues image uploads with three per environment, a five-minute timeout and HEIC decoding with a quality ladder; generic files use another path and no content hashing was found [SRC]. Sandbox data analysis is documentation only for claude.ai, ChatGPT, Manus and Perplexity [DOC]. Presigned direct upload to object storage, read-only mounts at a known path and per-call minute limits are Verevon design, not reference behaviour [INF].

**Verevon today.** Documents are extracted client-side, capped at 1 MB, 60,000 characters per file and 120,000 per turn, and inlined into message text (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/lib/prepare-chat-attachments.ts:6-43; chat-normalizers.ts:1150-1240). Only image parts are dropped on the ChatGPT subscription route (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/api/chat-client.ts:1144); text still reaches the model. An image diverts the whole turn, including a Do turn, into AnalyzeImage (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/sse.rs:1405-1432; vision.rs:1-60 uses the first image only). The composer has no MIME or capability check (DashboardComposer.tsx:1699-1725,3419-3430), although inference-core already emits vision and image feature flags (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/inference-core/src/provider/mod.rs:655-685). The durable upload route is text-only, asynchronous, refused under ZDR with 412 and has no production caller (chat-client.ts:2081-2118). code_interpreter files_in is model-authored base64 only (execution-core/src/code_interpreter.rs:180-260). Reuse ModelInfo.capabilities, the imports-core extract route and inference-core AnalyzeDocument OCR.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Phase 1 ships alone at S to M. For H6, bytes reach the sandbox through Data Plane or an ephemeral model-gateway store, never direct DB access, and a Do run receives only what model-gateway resolves into RunAgentRequest. Make durability an org-policy opt-in and hash content per org; do not wire the existing async text route as-is, because it recreates the failure client-side inlining fixed. Vision turns stay refused for waived ZDR turns (sse.rs:960-977). Feeds [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014). Risks: retention under ZDR, cross-tenant dedup leaking existence, deletion cascading to derived documents.

<a id="F-t3-018"></a>
### F-t3-018 · Scheduled runs become governed, tool-using Do runs with a stored approval contract, non-delegable tools, result delivery and origin attribution
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (orchestrator-core supervision, execution-core) with Control Plane (signed decisions) and Application Plane (delivery) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Replace the single tool-less completion in the scheduled step with a normal budgeted Do run started by the Temporal supervision through the same RunAgent path as chat Do. At schedule creation the owner signs a consent card; the approved capabilities and their expiry are stored in the Control-committed template so every fire and step can re-verify them. At run time an effect proceeds only when it is covered and delegable; otherwise the run drafts and notifies the owner off-surface, with no Allow action for high-risk tools. Add a working "See results" delivery and a run-level origin tag (chat, schedule, trigger).

**Why it matters.** Scheduled agents are near-universal among the references and the Agents pages already promise them, but each Verevon step today is a stateless 1,024-token completion that cannot use a tool. The owner ruling of 2026-09-28 (draft-only) stays compatible: the stored contract starts as read plus draft and widens only by explicit decision.

**What the references do.** Hermes runs cron jobs as full agent runs with skills, chained context and multi-platform delivery, backed by an at-most-once ledger (executions, occurrences, incidents, quota holds and delivery modules) [SRC]. Claude desktop stores approvedPermissions as {toolName, ruleContent?, approvedAt} per scheduled task; expiry is computed at check time from an admin-managed lifetime, and when that is unset approvals never expire [SRC]. Its shouldAutoApprovePermission refuses a fixed non-delegable set (Artifact-family, directory-mount, browser and computer, plugin-shim, save_skill and memory, scheduled-task tools), audits each auto-approval with the task id, and drops the Allow action from notifications for risky tools [SRC]. Hermes' deny-by-default in cron and unattended modes covers only the dangerous-command gate; other cron tools run auto-approved, and narrowed toolsets apply to the webhook surface or optional per-job toolsets [SRC]. Manus has run_mode (ask or full auto) and an expire_config per schedule; the autonomy cap here is a Verevon synthesis [SRC, INF]. The 10 of 12 references count double-counts Manus and Claude vendors. Caveats: off-surface approval is Claude desktop only (1 of 12 references); Codex and Cursor schedulers are server-side and seen only as client strings; DeepSeek scheduling is off by default on desktop [SRC, DOC].

**Verevon today.** Partial. The cron sweeper, the ScheduledRunSupervision workflow (apps/Model Plane/go/services/orchestrator-core/cmd/workflows/interactive_run.go:180-260) and schedule-from-chat exist, and Control-signed create and per-fire decisions carry permissions, expiry and a ZDR block (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/cron/control_authorizer.go:150-240). But each step is one tool-less completion (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/grpc.rs:498-530), an approval need fails the run (interactive_run.go:243), "See results" is disabled (ScheduledRunsPanel.tsx:125-145) and only threads carry an origin (migration 0034). Reuse the signed decisions, the scheduled_step_receipts ledger (migration 0029), the supervision cancel signal, the approval outbox, notification-core, ScheduleDialog and ScheduledRunsPanel.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Plan rows D3 to D11 already cover the read-only tool loop, result persistence, status, notification, identity and cadence guards; treat them as planned and unbuilt. Net-new here: the per-tool approved-capability contract with expiry in the Control template (after D2 digest parity), the non-delegable set from [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003), off-surface approval through Application Plane delivery because the workflow fails closed instead of pausing, and run-level origin. Authority stays in Control Plane; no Control bearer may persist in Temporal history; ZDR schedules stay blocked. Depends on [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001), [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002) and an ADR amending the draft-only ruling. Risks: stale consent, credential lifetime for late-starting runs, notification fatigue.

<a id="F-t3-019"></a>
### F-t3-019 · Real Agents and Automations hubs: versioned agent definitions, named agents, automations with run now and pause, and a working Fleet console
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | XL | Model Plane (runs) with Application Plane (agent definitions, ADR-0002) and Frontend Plane (hubs) | W3 | Medium |

**What to build.** Extend the existing Application Plane agent definition with version history, a tool allowlist bound to capability ids and an autonomy cap, and use it three ways: selectable roles for subagents, a builder with sandbox preview, and persistent named agents with their own thread and memory. Turn the agent pages from design previews into hubs over owner-plane resources, consolidate the two schedule UIs into one Automations hub over /api/v1/cron with Active, Paused and Completed filters and Run now, Pause and Edit, and either proxy the Fleet console to quarry-edge or remove it.

**Why it matters.** The audit is about the chat and agent pages, and those pages are largely blueprints: preset agents have empty action sets, the workflow builder and chatbot studio are previews, and the Fleet console calls a route no gateway serves. Users who are promised agents need something that runs.

**What the references do.** Perplexity's Automations dashboard lists tasks as Active, Paused or Completed with row actions Run now, Pause, Edit, Pin and Delete, Enable replacing Pause on paused rows, and up to 15 scheduled tasks per conversation; each hub is a list over one resource with a state filter and typed row actions [DOC]. Manus gives a named agent its own identity: the decoded agents protobuf carries email, phone number, a pc_id, payment enablement, connector and skill ids and an instruction, with Create, Update, Delete, CreateAgentEmail and SetAgentPinned RPCs, but no version field [SRC]. Cursor's named agents are team or admin managed and appear in the Glass agents window [SRC]. ChatGPT Workspace Agents are the only versioned source: a spec with publish and republish history, preview and an activity log, Business and Enterprise only [DOC, backed by Codex bundle strings SRC]. Codex's persistent assistant sits in a restricted namespace behind a Business Premium seat [SRC]; Hermes bots are documented over the profile primitive [DOC]. Prevalence: hubs 3 of 12 references; persistent named agents 5 of 12.

**Verevon today.** Partial. Schedules are managed in Settings (CronSchedulesSection.tsx:200-260) and chat (ScheduledRunsPanel.tsx:1-60) over cron-client.ts:28-110, but capability-core serves only list, create, get, patch and delete (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/api/workplane_apis.go:836-871), so there is no Run now and no run history. FleetRunConsole.tsx:15-60 fetches /v1/fleets/:id, which vite.config.ts:60-75 does not forward and only quarry-edge defines (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/fleet_routes.rs:1-15). Preset agents have empty action sets (preset-agents.ts:1-66); AgentsPage.tsx:285 carries the design-preview badge. Agent definitions already exist in Convex (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/schema.ts:324-384) with Space bindings, but unversioned with string tool names, and subagent.task has no role parameter (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:4265-4310). Reuse those tables, the gateway Space agent routes, CronHandler and ControlFireAuthorizer, the AgentRunConsole APIs and the artifact proxy pattern (json_handlers.rs:440-447).

**How to build it in Verevon.** Phase 1 (M): the Automations hub over existing PATCH and list, plus a Control-authorised run-now endpoint and a fires listing in capability-core, because Run now must pass the same Control fire authorisation and not call StartWorkflow directly; a Fleet proxy behind a Quarry-audience BFF, or removal. Phase 2: versioned definitions and the builder in Convex, consumed by Model Plane at invoke time as inject_mentioned_space_agent_persona already does, with action sets from [F-t3-025](#F-t3-025). Phase 3: persistent named agents with scoped memory from [F-t3-060](#F-t3-060), after [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018). Named agents need an explicit authority model for whose delegated token runs and must never widen an owner-plane ACL. Risks: scope creep into a workflow product; a second definition store if anything lands in capability-core.

<a id="F-t3-020"></a>
### F-t3-020 · User and org memory controls: on, pause, reset and org policy
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (session-core enforcement) with Control Plane (settings and org policy) and Frontend Plane (controls) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Add a user memory state (on, paused, off) and a Reset all memory action, plus an org policy that can disable memory and the learning review, enforced in session-core at every place memory is written or read: the Dreaming extractor, prefetch, save_memory, recall_memory and the skill review. Add an optional in-chat "remember this" path in Ask mode that confirms the save and is refused under ZDR (zero-data-retention).

**Why it matters.** Users and enterprise admins expect to stop the assistant learning about them, and a product that learns by default needs this before Norwegian and EU buyers can sign. Today a user can view, correct and delete entries but cannot switch learning off or wipe it in one action, and the stored Control Plane setting says off while the Model Plane learns anyway.

**What the references do.** Claude keeps topic-based entries saved during chat, each visible and editable, with remember and forget in chat, a separate pause (rows kept, no use and no generation) and a reset that erases; Team and Enterprise owners enable memory and members opt in, and turning it off at org level deletes all memory data [DOC, SRC]. Desktop strings confirm a transcript indicator when memory is read or written ("Reading memory...", "Saved to memory"), and both the legacy nightly-summary mode and the topic-entry mode ship in the strings [SRC]. Perplexity Enterprise admins manage memory org-wide and memories belong to the org [DOC]. ChatGPT, Antigravity (view, edit, delete) and Codex (reset, import; config-driven and possibly feature-gated) have user memory controls; Cursor has no account memory store [DOC, SRC]. Prevalence: 8 of 12 references at most; a distinct pause control is Claude only; org governance is 2 distinct vendors. Caveat carried into ZDR: claude.ai memory is unavailable to orgs with HIPAA, public-sector or custom-retention agreements [DOC].

**Verevon today.** Partial. Users can list, correct and delete (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/memory.rs:48-60; MemorySection.tsx:1-80), but user_memory_controls holds only extract_after and forgotten ids (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/migrations/0039_memory_user_control.sql:1-15), Dreaming is configured by env only (dreaming.rs:135-160) and the recall gate checks only conversation_only, web-only scope and trivial prompts (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/sse.rs:1648-1680). user-core stores memoryEnabled (default false) and personalizationEnabled (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/http/handlers.go:1395-1430) but nothing reads them. Ask turns cannot save (tool_loop.rs:625-642); ZDR turns neither read nor write (zdr_features.rs:150-185). Reuse memory_erasure.rs and gdpr.rs:440-456 for Reset across both tiers, the BFF stamp precedent apply_org_zdr_posture (chat/shared.rs:95-117) and the org_quota.rs service-principal pull.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Keep preference and org policy in Control Plane (CLAUDE.md:23) and make session-core user_memory_controls the single enforcement copy, written through a gateway route; stamp the decision on chat turns via the BFF and pull it with a service principal for Dreaming and the learning review, which run without a request. Settle the memoryEnabled=false versus learning-on default with a deliberate migration before shipping. Treat ZDR orgs as memory-off for writes. Risk: a pause that does not also stop the extractor and the skill review produces memories while paused.

**Quick win.** A user-level switch plus Reset is M on existing tables, erasure code and UI.

<a id="F-t3-021"></a>
### F-t3-021 · Transcript performance: benchmark first, then block-level streaming render, a virtualised timeline and paged transcript reads
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Frontend Plane (renderer, benchmark, chunk self-heal) with Model Plane (paged reads) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Add a DOM-level long-transcript render benchmark and a cold-start request budget in Playwright. Then ship the planned block-level incremental Markdown render (re-parse only the trailing block, frame-batched deltas), reload once on an unrecoverable chunk error, and, only if the benchmark shows a problem, virtualise the message list with a live-follow rule and a scroll-to-end pill and add tail-first paged transcript reads.

**Why it matters.** Long agent runs are where Verevon wants to win, and today every streamed token re-parses the whole message and remounts every block, and nothing is virtualised. Users notice the jank immediately.

**What the references do.** T3 Code renders the timeline with LegendList (estimatedItemSize 90, maintainScrollAtEnd), re-arms follow when the user is within 40 px of the end, and caps hidden mounts at 10 and 3 [SRC]. DeepSeek harness freezes all but the trailing two blocks (UNSTABLE_TAIL_BLOCKS = 2, frozen prefix), resumes the Shiki grammar state line by line, and skips colouring lines over 1,000 characters [SRC]. Claude desktop reads transcripts tail-first and in pages over IPC and a MessagePort (getTranscriptTail, getTranscriptPage) [SRC]; its 257-module tiered lazy registry with an onUnrecoverable reload is a boot and chunk-resilience point, not transcript evidence [SRC]. DeepSeek's startup test budgets only settings/describe calls (budget 2), not all requests [SRC]. ChatGPT paged loading is a release note [DOC]; Antigravity virtualisation rests on a TanStack Virtual polyfill note and a diff worker, medium confidence [SRC]. No reference benchmarks transcript rendering, so "benchmark first" is a Verevon recommendation. Prevalence 4 of 12 references.

**Verevon today.** Partial. Route-level lazy loading (App.tsx:8-40) and a unit-level per-token budget (src/shared/chat-nodes/derive.budget.test.ts:1-40) exist. But ChatMarkdown reparses and remounts per token (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/components/ChatMessages.tsx:1085-1110, 1205-1250; chat-media-markdown.tsx:745-790), all turns sit in one un-virtualised list (ChatMessages.tsx:669), and reads are unpaged: ListConversationRequest has no limit or cursor (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/sessions.proto:901-918; http_routes.rs:8470-8535; history.rs:590-645). No vite:preloadError self-heal. Reuse the existing autoFollow, handleScroll and scroll-to-latest button (use-chat-controller.ts:1954-1966), the parseMarkdownBlocks closed flag on fences, and tests/e2e/product-phase-timing.ts stamps for the budget.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Sequence: (1) benchmark plus cold-start budget, (2) plan I5 block-level render, (3) preloadError reload-once, (4) virtualisation and paged reads conditional on measurement, as the workspace plan already says (VEREVON_CHAT_WORKSPACE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md:435). Drop the worker-highlighting sub-item: the highlighter is a small regex scanner already skipped above 120,000 characters (chat-code-highlight.ts:1-40). Paging is net-new across session-core, model-gateway and the BFF; read canonical_messages_to_transcript before designing tail-first paging because the SPA keeps a local snapshot (INF, from history.rs naming). No ZDR or Space-authority impact. Risks: variable-height content causes scroll jumps; selection, find and screen readers must survive virtualisation.

**Quick win.** Benchmark, I5 and the reload-once fix are M and purely Frontend Plane.

<a id="F-t3-022"></a>
### F-t3-022 · Prompt-cache discipline: measured hit rate, stable/context/volatile prompt tiers with an append-only tail, lineage-scoped cache keys and compaction transactions
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Model Plane (model-gateway prompt assembly, inference-core providers, cost-core) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Build an aggregate cache hit-rate view over the cache tokens cost-core already stores, add a miss-reason field, and run the flagged stable-prefix order on a staging OpenAI or Azure deployment with eval reruns. Then define a prompt-tier contract in mp-contracts for both loops: org and Space blocks in the shared prefix, per-user memory and volatile injections (time, recall, skills, approved plan) in an append-only tail; lineage-scoped cache keys; todo recitation in the tail; the Anthropic two-block system split; and compaction as a bracketed transaction with a visible marker.

**Why it matters.** Cache hits have never been measured on any route, so Verevon cannot tell whether it pays full price for every agent round. Prefix stability is a precondition for adding more context to Do runs in [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001).

**What the references do.** Hermes builds a three-tier system prompt (stable, context, volatile) once per session and rebuilds it only on compression, so the longest prefix stays byte-identical [SRC]. Its cache scope is the root of the compression lineage: /new starts fresh, forks are isolated, and a hash keys the scope [SRC]. It places four cache_control breakpoints, keeps an api_content sidecar so wire-only recall replays identically, re-injects the todo store after compression under a fixed header, and counts OpenRouter cache HIT headers [SRC]. DeepSeek harness appends runtime facts only on change through a RuntimeContextProjection, models compaction as a transaction with an unmatched-start state, and /compact reports estimated tokens saved; its README says measured hit rates are not guaranteed [SRC, DOC]. Cursor uses a rolling four-breakpoint rule and prefix-reusing summary calls [SRC]. Manus' KV-cache lessons and todo recitation are blog posts [DOC]. No reference exposes a hit-rate KPI; that is a Verevon recommendation. Prevalence 5 of 12 references.

**Verevon today.** Partial. Measurement plumbing is built end to end: inference-core parses cache read and write tokens for Anthropic (anthropic.rs:823), OpenAI and Azure (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/inference-core/src/provider/openai.rs:371-395), cost-core persists and sums them (apps/Model Plane/go/services/cost-core/internal/postgres/store.go:153, 195-210), the SPA has a per-turn Prompt cache metric (ChatPanels.tsx:926-940) that reads zero because the Usage frame carries no cache fields (sse_events.rs:268-286; see [F-t3-028](#F-t3-028)) and the Do loop logs them per round (agent.rs:1448-1466). Missing: an aggregate view and miss reasons, the off-by-default order (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/prompt_context.rs:1-60), the Anthropic split (anthropic.rs:296-345 joins one system block), the tier contract, lineage keys, todo recitation (CHAT_PARITY_AUDIT_2026-10-01.md:110) and compaction anchoring. Reuse PromptContext and render_stable_prefix, compaction.rs and SUMMARY_STABILITY_CLAUSE, cost_entries, the context inspector route and orchestration_store todos.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Metrics shrink to S because the data exists. Put the Anthropic split in inference-core with a gateway-signalled boundary (audit section 6.2), since reordering alone is a no-op there. Land the tier contract before or with [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001). Inside a Do run the fold is already append-only (agent.rs:1185-1226), so the contract is first a gateway chat-loop problem. Constraints: provider-side caching must pass the per-provider ZDR contract, ZDR telemetry stays numeric-only, and the response cache already excludes ZDR (cache.rs:1-10). Add a CI check that volatile sections never precede the breakpoint. Risk: the audit found an identical question gave a different prompt hash (CACHE_COMPACTION_AUDIT_2026-09-17.md:85-100), and the order's answer-quality effect is unmeasured.

**Quick win.** The hit-rate view and the staging measurement need no new telemetry.

<a id="F-t3-023"></a>
### F-t3-023 · Org and role model entitlements: Control Plane decides, Model Plane enforces at admission
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Absent | L | Control Plane (org-core policy) with Model Plane (model-gateway enforcement) and Frontend Plane (admin UI) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Give the refused allowed_models field a real owner: an org-core entitlement policy (org, role, group, plan) with allow and deny model lists, a maximum effort, an admin default model and effort per surface, and an explain endpoint for one member's effective access. model-gateway pulls the resolved entitlement, filters list_models, and refuses a disallowed model at admission with a typed reason, never silently substituting.

**Why it matters.** Enterprise buyers expect to restrict expensive or non-EU models per role. The code flags the hole itself: SetPolicy refuses the allowlist by name because no service owns it, so operators cannot restrict models per org at all today.

**What the references do.** ChatGPT Enterprise and Edu admins set a starting model, reasoning level and speed separately per surface, and some recent models launch off by default; model access across roles is additive (a role that allows a model keeps it enabled) while Lockdown roles override, and a Model Test tool shows one member's effective access [DOC; live help pages returned 403, verified against cached copies]. Claude desktop strings show per-role effort caps where the most permissive maximum across a member's roles applies; the enforcement path was not traced [SRC]. Codex gates models by plan lists in its catalogue data, which is not org-role policy [SRC]. T3 Code gates by provider CLI version and does not count. Corrected prevalence: 4 of 12 references, common rather than table-stakes; the admin features are Enterprise and Edu plan-gated.

**Verevon today.** Absent. OrgPolicy.allowed_models has no owning service and is refused (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/grpc.rs:148-160; apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/gateway.proto:1335-1357). The only live allowlist is the deployment-wide ALLOWED_MODELS env var (normalize.rs:360-384); list_models ignores caller claims (http_routes.rs:7590-7640) and inference-core ListModels takes only modality and provider (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/inference-core/src/grpc.rs:394-424). The Convex mirror fields allowedModels and defaultModel are never read for admission (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/schema.ts:14-23). Reuse the org-core org_entitlements table and GET /orgs/{id}/entitlements with the session-core entitlements_changed aggregation (apps/Control Plane/org-core/internal/org/types.go:266-291; repository.go:663-667; session-core org_client.go:124-148), the org_quota::fetch_org_limits service-principal pull (org_quota.rs:1-60), the 60-second retentionPosture claim precedent (apps/Control Plane/auth-core/src/auth/convex-token.service.ts:150-215), the normalize.rs choke point, and the router-policy admin page as UI neighbour.

**How to build it in Verevon.** CLAUDE.md:23 assigns entitlements to Control Plane, so storage, role resolution and explain live in org-core (Go); roles are not in mp-authctx Claims (claims.rs:1-60), so org-core resolves role to allowlist before Model Plane sees it. Enforce in model-gateway, with inference-core chain selection as defence in depth beside the privacy-tier floor. Deliver by short-TTL service-principal pull or a compact policy-version claim. Fail closed: org_quota fails open on an org-core outage (sse.rs:1154-1155), which is wrong for a deny list; use last-known-good with a short TTL or refuse. Role-check writes in org-core, because the forwarded x-user-role header (router_policy.rs:1-60) is not sufficient authority. An entitlement only narrows access and never widens a Space or owner-plane ACL; for a ZDR org it must not strip every ZDR-attested model, so admission refuses with a typed reason. Needs an ADR on entitlement ownership. Risk: stale cached entitlements allowing a revoked model.

**Quick win.** An org-level allow and deny list with default model and a list_models filter is M on the org_quota pull pattern.

## 5. Recommended

Recommended items are worth building but are not the first bets. Most sit on top of a Priority item (the sandbox computer, the capability gate, the browser run) or restate a slice of an existing Verevon plan. Verification changed several of them: some baselines were richer than first described, some efforts moved, and several bundles should be split into independent slices. Each card states the verified gap with code citations and the adjusted approach.

<a id="F-t3-024"></a>
### F-t3-024 · Computer use inside the agent's own sandbox desktop (consent tiers only if user-desktop control is ever unparked)
*Verevon today: Absent · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane (execution-core computer tool, guest agent); Endpoint Plane only for phase 2 · Wave: W4 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Add a computer tool whose Model-proposed actions (screenshot plus accessibility tree, click, type, scroll) are executed by a guest agent on a desktop inside the sandbox VM, with a receipt per action. "Controls a computer" is part of the vision; doing it in the agent's own VM respects the owner's decision not to drive users' desktops.

**Reference practice.** Desktop computer use ships in 8 of 12 references; consent tiers are a differentiator (2 of 12). Manus returns an accessibility tree with short handles and an optional screenshot with a capture id, acts by handle or coordinates plus capture id, and refuses ambiguous targets [SRC]. Claude adds per-app consent tiers, a deny list, a global lock and restoration at turn end [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** No desktop tool exists: Do mode offers only browser.observe/browser.act and code_interpreter (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:4190-4222) on a one-shot bubblewrap child (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/sandbox.rs:255-310); the Endpoint Plane agent is Windows-only and untargeted. Order: (1) S, now: write the typed connector, then Quarry browser, then screen hierarchy into the Do prompt and capability policy; (2) after [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009) and [F-t3-035](#F-t3-035), extend that typed executor protocol with a desktop surface modelled on Quarry's snapshot-scoped refs and step receipts; (3) keep the coordinate fallback off until an ADR.

**Dependencies and risks.** Nearly all cost sits in [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009); user-desktop control stays parked; on-screen prompt injection and vision cost.

<a id="F-t3-025"></a>
### F-t3-025 · Open the typed action registry to the agent: classify every action, admit reads first, mint per-run scoped tokens
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Frontend Plane (registry) with the Model Plane capability gate and each owning plane · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Classify every typed action by model access (none, read, write-with-approval) and risk, admit reads per owning plane through the capability gate, then writes under exact-effect approval, with per-run scoped authority. This is the core of the AI-first thesis: the agent uses the same actions and guards as the UI.

**Reference practice.** 4 of 12 references expose their own product to the agent. Claude desktop ships built-in MCP (Model Context Protocol) servers for sessions, sidebar and settings, and each Preferences key declares whether the model may read or change it [SRC]. T3 Code issues a per-session scoped bearer stored only as a hash [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** ActionDescriptor already carries risk, requiresApproval and reversible (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/actions/types.ts:15-24), but isModelExecutableAction admits 8 of 168 actions (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/actions/model-eligibility.ts:126-140). Control already signs per-run, per-action authority, yet runActionTarget accepts only tickets.create (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/run_action_decision.go:340-347). Generalise run-action-v1 and model-action-view-v1 into a per-action table keyed by the catalog schema hash; Control mints the authority, the BFF keeps minting only per-audience user bearers. Add a model-access class and exempt reason to ActionDescriptor and turn the catalog-manifest allowlist test into the CI ratchet, following plan milestones M2 and M3.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003), [F-t3-006](#F-t3-006); the decision needs a non-ZDR run and Space evidence; each admission is its own M of owner-plane work.

<a id="F-t3-026"></a>
### F-t3-026 · Human takeover and hand-back from live views: agent-requested takeover for logins, 2FA and CAPTCHAs, then point-and-comment steering
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Model Plane (pause and resume) with Ingestion Plane (Quarry control mode) and Frontend Plane (takeover card, live view) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Let the agent ask for help when a browser run hits a login, two-factor prompt or CAPTCHA, hand the hosted browser to the user, and resume with the user's note and a fresh snapshot; human-typed input never reaches the model or replays. A second track lets the user steer by clicking an element or drawing a region.

**Reference practice.** 5 of 12 references hand control to the human and back. Manus shows agent-suggested takeover cards (view, take over, pause, exit takeover) and a hand-back message [DOC]. Cursor's design mode lets the user click elements or draw a region in the in-app browser and comment [SRC]; point-and-comment is a differentiator (2 of 12).

**Verevon today and approach.** Manual takeover exists: Quarry owns the control mode (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/step_receipts.rs:55-95) and the BFF returns 409 to agent actions while a human holds control (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:690-715). ChatLiveRunPanel mounts BrowserChrome with pause and resume but passes no onBrowserControlMode, so the toggle is disabled (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/BrowserChrome.tsx:621-628), and Quarry's ChallengeSignal is not consumed. Approach: (1) wire takeover and hand-back into ChatLiveRunPanel, closing plan E10; (2) add a human_assist kind on the existing durable approval gate, triggered by ChallengeSignal or a planner action, as a proposal Quarry accepts, with a longer wait budget; (3) point-and-comment later on the [F-t3-031](#F-t3-031) annotation part. Drop synthetic-input arbitration; Quarry's single control-mode state already covers it.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008), [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002); any login carry-over grant needs an expiry.

<a id="F-t3-027"></a>
### F-t3-027 · Deep research v2: clarifying questions and an editable plan, mid-run steering, a gap-driven second pass with a claim ledger, and knowledge plus connector sources in one cited report
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Split deep research into an editable plan step, a second pass that targets gaps and conflicts through a claim-to-source ledger, and one report that cites web, knowledge and connector sources. Plan transparency and gap-driven passes make research trustworthy at depth.

**Reference practice.** Reference evidence is documentation only. ChatGPT deep research moves through clarify, plan proposed, user edit, running and an incrementally streamed report, and a changed site list mid-run re-plans the rest [DOC]. Codex's deep-research skill loops plan, broad read, claim ledger, gap queries and a diminishing-returns stop [DOC]. Plan editing was recorded for 3 of 11 sources with research records, second passes for 5 of 11 [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** run_deep_research (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/deep_research.rs:2413-2470) is one in-stream, web-only pass: up to 9 sub-queries shown as a checklist, about 16 pages read, citations before synthesis, a mandatory could-not-verify section. There is no clarification, plan approval, ledger or second pass, and sse.rs:945-985 rejects knowledge_only scope with deep research. The pipeline dies with its SSE stream, so no gate can wait inside it. Ship three slices: a plan turn returning an editable sub-query node, then a run turn re-clamped server-side; an in-process second pass driven by the existing coverage shortfall inside the wall clock; knowledge and connector sources through existing viewer-ACL retrieval under retrieval_scope. Demote mid-run steering: queued_input only lands at tool-round boundaries.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t3-054](#F-t3-054), [F-t3-010](#F-t3-010); refused under ZDR, so no ledger persists there; extra passes raise synthesis cost.

<a id="F-t3-028"></a>
### F-t3-028 · Live run-cost meter with pre-run estimate, cost by token category, quota display and allowance warnings
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane (cost-core, model-gateway events) with Control Plane (quotas) and Frontend Plane · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Emit cumulative usage progress after every provider call, show a live meter in the Work panel and Run Console, warn at 80 percent of the run or org ceiling, and later show a labelled estimate on the plan card. Verevon's honesty contract (cost null when unknown, basis marks subscription use) is extended, not replaced.

**Reference practice.** A live meter and estimate is a differentiator (2 of 12); an allowance page is table-stakes (7 of 12). Manus records credits per reply, states roughly how many credits a long operation will consume, keeps a credit ledger and pre-checks balance per metered tool [SRC]. Antigravity shows cost by input, cache read, output, thinking and tools per turn and subagent [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** ChatEvent::Usage fires once at turn end without cache fields (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/sse_events.rs:268-286), although the client reads cache_read_tokens (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/api/chat-client.ts:335-350) and the Prompt cache metric appears to stay at zero [INF]. Already present: the cost-core ledger with a per-run aggregate, org allowance page, quotas with reset period, and a silent 80 percent budget posture that routes one tier cheaper (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/inference-core/src/provider/routing_policy.rs:126-134). Start from plan I16 (UsageProgress frames plus warning), add cache fields to the Usage frame, and surface the silent downgrade in the same warning. Drop disjoint reasoning tokens (providers count them inside output); defer attribution, thread totals and the estimate. On ZDR turns compute the meter in-stream, since zdr ledger envelopes are suppressed.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t3-022](#F-t3-022), [F-t3-042](#F-t3-042); per-user allowance needs Control Plane billing support; estimates stay labelled.

<a id="F-t3-029"></a>
### F-t3-029 · Canonical reasoning-effort ladder mapped per model to each provider wire format
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Model Plane · Wave: W1 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Define one canonical effort scale and one resolve step in inference-core that every adapter calls: clamp to the nearest weaker level the model supports, then map to the provider vocabulary. The Budget and Balance defaults are mostly OpenAI-family models, and that route ignores the user's depth choice.

**Reference practice.** 6 of 12 references implement a ladder. Hermes keeps an EFFORT_LADDER with per-generation wire vocabularies and a clamp that picks the nearest weaker supported level instead of sending a value that returns 400, caching capability probes per model and base URL [SRC]. Codex declares a per-model ladder with descriptions, DeepSeek refuses unsupported values before dispatch, T3 Code renders controls from descriptors [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Effort is a three-value profile (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/thinking.rs:1-80) that becomes an Anthropic thinking budget or a Codex low or high effort only. The OpenAI and Azure builder sends no reasoning effort even for gpt-5 and o-series ids (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/inference-core/src/provider/openai.rs:430-455), and the Do loop hard-sets thinking_budget_tokens to 0 (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:1334-1340). Start with the smallest correct slice: derive OpenAI and Azure reasoning_effort from the existing budget as codex_reasoning_effort does, for gpt-5 and o-series ids only. The six-level ladder needs an additive InferRequest proto field and composer levels, so it is step two. Keep the per-model table in inference-core; effort applies only to the final answer inference, not tool rounds.

**Dependencies and risks.** Effort changes cost, so defaults tie to budget posture; a per-role cap is a Control-owned entitlement; extending effort to Do runs is a product decision.

<a id="F-t3-030"></a>
### F-t3-030 · Workspace file tools for Do mode: versioned read, grep, glob and list, plus governed edit and write on one small Rust edit engine
*Verevon today: Absent · Effort: M · Plane: Model Plane (execution-core tools, capability-core ids) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add bounded read tools over the hydrated Space workspace (ranged read with line numbers and a content hash, ripgrep-shaped grep, glob, list) and governed edit and write tools on one Rust edit engine with unique-match replace and a stale-read guard. Today agents change workspace files only by writing a whole program through code_interpreter.

**Reference practice.** Every coding-capable reference ships an edit tool; detailed records exist for 3 of the 9 references with coding records. Cursor's StrReplace uses a matching ladder, a structured result and a per-file lock; its grep maps onto bundled ripgrep with context lines and a truncated flag and refuses an unbounded broad glob [SRC]. DeepSeek adds a stale-read version guard with a remedy message [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** No read, grep, glob, edit or write tool exists in either catalogue (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:4090,4190-4222). Changes made through code_interpreter are diffed against a CAS (content-addressed storage) baseline and merged by sandbox-manager Promote with a per-path base_hash compare-and-swap (apps/Model Plane/go/services/sandbox-manager/internal/workspace/store.go:100-175). Implement reads first inside execution-core over the hydrated root, keep diff_and_upload and Promote as the only durable path, and derive the per-file stale token from the CAS baseline hash so both guards agree. Add the new ids explicitly to permission::is_risky_tool (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/permission/mod.rs:229-275, a substring match fs.edit would miss) and to trusted_capability_id. Fix the redaction hash-domain issue first, since diff_and_upload hashes redacted bytes.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003); durable only for Space-scoped runs until [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009)'s thread workspace; ZDR runs write nothing durable.

<a id="F-t3-031"></a>
### F-t3-031 · Artifact canvas: server-side versions with restore, user editing, selection comments and selection-scoped AI edits, annotation, then collaborative documents
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane (version store) with Frontend Plane (editor), Application Plane (realtime) and Data Plane v2 (promotion) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Let users edit, restore and point at parts of an artifact in the Output panel, then add selection comments and annotation, and later collaborative documents. Canvas-style editing is how claude.ai, ChatGPT and Manus let users finish work with the agent.

**Reference practice.** Versioned artifacts with restore are common (4 of 12); selection comments, annotation and collaborative documents are differentiators (2 of 12 each). Manus keeps a server-side version list with restore and editor attribution (AI, owner, collaborator) [SRC]. T3 Code serialises a review comment with path, range, text and diff excerpt that round-trips into a comment card [SRC]. Claude desktop ships a Rust-to-WASM yrs CRDT (conflict-free replicated data type) document engine [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The gateway already owns the version counter and current text and serialises run-versus-run commits with lock_commit and unchanged_from (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/artifacts.rs:137-232). There is no revision store with its own ids, no route to read, write or restore, no editor attribution, and the panel's version stepper is read-only (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/components/ChatArtifactPanel.tsx:195-345); no selection handling exists in verevonv3/src. Phase 1 is plan I17: client-side highlight-to-edit through the normal model revision path and restore by re-emitting an old revision as a new version; OD19 rules out a server range-patch API. A later user-save RPC goes through the existing lock_commit path with an editor attribute. Annotation (M) and yrs documents (XL) are separate later bets.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012), [F-t3-052](#F-t3-052); ZDR threads persist no artifacts; save to knowledge must create a Data Plane document under the user's token.

<a id="F-t3-032"></a>
### F-t3-032 · Org agent policy registry: named agent modes, setting locks, a lockdown profile, extension allow-lists, frozen tool snapshots, durable hooks and one Customize hub
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Control Plane (policy registry) with Model Plane (capability-core enforcement, model-gateway mode clamp) and Frontend Plane (hub) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add named agent modes clamped to min(org cap, user choice), a signed tighten-only org policy registry with locks and a lockdown profile, frozen tool snapshots after admin approval, durable hooks that reach Do runs, and one Customize hub. The enforcement core exists; hook rules are in memory and admins cannot set modes or locks.

**Reference practice.** Named modes ship in 8 of 12 references and admin registries in 7 of 12; lockdown is a differentiator (3 of 12). Codex uses a typed mode vocabulary with per-effect approval config and a full-access confirmation that lists risks [SRC]. Claude keeps a schema-as-source registry with a fail-closed value per key, tighten-only remote config and 33 hook events [SRC]. ChatGPT freezes a custom app's tool list after admin approval [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Only PermissionMode Auto/Ask/Deny (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/permission/mod.rs:6-58; from_wire maps unknown strings to Auto), a server-floored posture and an AutonomyRung grant exist, with no org cap. The hook engine is real but HookRegistry is in-memory and the Do loop passes an empty hook_context (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:1900-1930); a durable hook_configs table exists in session-core (migrations/0004_tasks_cron_hooks_skills_memory.sql:160-185) but only erasure touches it. Admin sections for MCP servers, plugins, skills and org instructions already exist. Split: plan B4 (S) passes hook_context into Do runs; durable hooks plus admin API (M); AgentMode mapped onto PermissionMode plus AutonomyRung (M); Control-signed registry, locks and lockdown (L); snapshots pinning name, description and schema hashes, plus the hub (M).

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003), [F-t3-006](#F-t3-006); owner-plane effects are never auto-approved; new modes must not ride from_wire until it fails closed.

<a id="F-t3-033"></a>
### F-t3-033 · Content-safety hardening: an injection normaliser, enforcing semantic screening for web and browser content, one policy check on every memory and skill write, and safe links
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane (model-gateway moderation, session-core memory) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add a Unicode normaliser before the injection scan, make semantic screening enforcing for external web and browser content after a false-positive evaluation, run one deterministic policy on every memory and skill write, and add safe-link handling. Exposure grows as browser and connector reads arrive.

**Reference practice.** Injection defences are table-stakes (8 of 12). Perplexity documents the most layered stack: intent anchoring, a screening classifier, containment and runtime monitoring [DOC]. Claude's scrubber normalises text, strips the U+E0000 tag block and zero-width characters, and neutralises lookalike angle brackets and reserved envelope tags [SRC]. Hermes stages memory and skill writes and scans installs against a trust matrix [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Shipped: trust-class provenance, a fail-closed full-payload scan, hash-only security events and a 20 percent sampled semantic shadow pass (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/semantic_screening.rs:1-60,93-116) wired only into the chat tool loop. scan_injection substring-matches 16 phrases with no normalisation, the Do-mode seam gets only the marker scan (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/mod.rs:795-820), IndexMemory checks nothing beyond non-empty content (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/memory_grpc.rs:444-500), and learned skills are upserted unscanned. Order: (1) a shared normaliser crate used by both scanner copies, with a test corpus; (2) a deterministic memory policy at IndexMemory and extractor output with a typed refusal; (3) enforcing posture for ExternalWeb and Browser after the evaluation, chat first, Do mode needing a new pass; (4) a chat link scheme allowlist reusing ooxml.ts isSafeExportHref; (5) skill-write scan before promotion is enabled.

**Dependencies and risks.** Evaluation set from shadow telemetry; semantic screening may only tighten posture and is skipped under ZDR; never log flagged content.

<a id="F-t3-034"></a>
### F-t3-034 · Trajectory ledger with exact model-visible request capture and timing waterfall
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane (session-core, model-gateway, execution-core) and Frontend Plane (Trace tab) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add a second view of every run: a virtualised ledger grouped by turn, step, request and tool call with a per-record inspector, an Inspect action on each tool card, request snapshots written at the model call site, and a timing waterfall drawn only from recorded timestamps. Nobody can see today what the model was actually sent, which also blocks replay tests.

**Reference practice.** A ledger is a differentiator (2 of 12); request capture is common (4 of 12). DeepSeek keeps the invariant that anything model-visible is logged: one append-only per-session log, a request header event appended only when it changes, a diffable system-prompt event, auxiliary requests logged before dispatch, and a waterfall from recorded timestamps only [SRC]. Antigravity adds queue and approval time to timing; Cursor exposes copyable request ids [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The Trace tab is a flat projection capped at 5,000 events (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/components/ChatPanels.tsx:1400-1466); per-turn timing is content-free and not persisted per step (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/turn_timing.rs:1-40). No snapshot of the exact system prompt, tool catalogue or parameters exists, and the gateway trajectory store is an in-memory ring buffer for training export. Order by risk: (1) group existing run events by turn, step and action_id with an inspector drawer and copy trace id, needing no new storage; (2) persist step timing as content-free events; (3) request capture last, short-retention, tenant-keyed, hashes only under ZDR, wired into thread delete, Space deletion and GDPR purge.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t3-049](#F-t3-049), ZDR resolver; owner-only access, no ACL widening; which platform prompt text is shown is a product decision.

<a id="F-t3-035"></a>
### F-t3-035 · Live computer panel with typed viewers (browser, read-only terminal, files, later desktop) and model-facing PTY exec sessions
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Frontend Plane (panel) with Model Plane (execution-core process host) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Turn ChatLiveRunPanel into a computer panel that picks a viewer by tool kind: browser frames, a read-only terminal fed by redacted process output, file and diff viewers, later a desktop. Give the model a PTY (pseudo-terminal) option with long output spilled to workspace files. This is how a user watches the agent work.

**Reference practice.** A computer panel and terminal panes each ship in 4 of 12 references; output offload is a differentiator (2 of 12).
