### 9.2 Quick wins

Twelve items carry the quick-win flag. Nine of them stay at S or M after verification; the table lists them with the reason they are cheap. Several are "finish what is built but dark": the code exists and is not deployed, not enabled or not measured.

| ID | Title | Effort | Wave | Why it is cheap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001) | One server-assembled run context | M | W1 | The BFF already injects org, Space and persona text onto the agentic request and model-gateway composes it for Ask turns; the agentic branch simply returns before composition (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/sse.rs:1405-1500), so the fix is one composed field on RunAgentRequest plus bounded history. |
| [F-t3-005](#F-t3-005) | Unblock Zero Data Retention | M | W1 | The design is built; the attestation itself is setting environment fields and a digest. The M comes from collecting contract evidence, deploying the uncommitted waves in order and running two live passes. |
| [F-t3-021](#F-t3-021) | Transcript performance | M | W1 | Part of the benchmark exists at unit level and plan I5 (block-level streaming render) is already specified; virtualisation and paged reads are conditional follow-ups. |
| [F-t3-022](#F-t3-022) | Prompt-cache discipline | M | W1 | Cache telemetry is already stored, so the metrics step shrinks to S; the OpenAI stable-prefix order is built and only needs enabling and an eval rerun. |
| [F-t3-029](#F-t3-029) | Canonical reasoning-effort ladder | M (was S) | W1 | A well-understood code gap: the OpenAI-family path ignores the user's effort choice. S for that slice; M only because the full ladder needs a proto field and three adapters. |
| [F-t3-039](#F-t3-039) | Runtime release safety | M | W1 | Signing, rollback-artifact validation and a conformance gate are built; what remains is thresholds, a rehearsal script, a migration policy and operator deployment with keys. |
| [F-t3-025](#F-t3-025) | Open the typed action registry to the agent | M | W2 | Risk metadata and per-run scoped authority exist in part; the framework tranche is classification fields, a CI ratchet and generalising the existing Control decision. Each further action admission is separate owner-plane work. |
| [F-t3-030](#F-t3-030) | Workspace file tools for Do mode | M | W2 | The hydrated Space workspace and CAS (content-addressed storage) merge are real; read, grep and glob are S and only the guarded edit engine is M. |
| [F-t3-050](#F-t3-050) | Scrubbable browser-run replay | M | W4 | Step timeline, rationale and screenshots already exist as events; the scrubber alone is S and the clip is assembled from existing screenshots. |

Three flagged items rose to L during verification and are no longer whole-item quick wins, but each has a cheap first slice: [F-t3-004](#F-t3-004) Phase 0 (deploy the built audit outbox fix) is S, while the hash chain, compliance API and SIEM (security information and event management) export are M to L; [F-t3-020](#F-t3-020) is M for a user-level on/paused/off switch plus reset, with org policy and learning-review gating making it L; [F-t3-023](#F-t3-023) is M for an org allow/deny list on the existing org_quota pull pattern, with role and plan resolution making it L.

### 9.3 Decisions needed before building (ADR candidates)

Ten topics touch standing rules (plane ownership, ZDR propagation, Space authority, "the Model proposes and Quarry executes", no LLM on the permission path). Each needs an ADR or an owner ruling before implementation starts.

1. **Sandbox computer backend and thread-scoped workspace.** Question: does Verevon move from hermetic per-call bwrap processes to long-lived microVMs with a thread-scoped workspace, and under what retention, ACL and billing rules? Items: [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009), [F-t3-024](#F-t3-024), [F-t3-035](#F-t3-035), [F-t3-043](#F-t3-043), [F-t3-084](#F-t3-084), [F-t3-085](#F-t3-085). Options: keep bwrap and add only a thread workspace on it (phase 1, about M); or add a SandboxBackend seam and a microVM backend (self-hosted Firecracker or Kata, or an attested managed provider). The decision ledger of 2026-08-22 rejects a SandboxBackend trait until a second real backend is planned, so the ADR must reverse that first, show ZDR snapshot destruction, prove that a thread workspace never aliases or bypasses Space ACLs, name the owner of guest images and define VM-second metering. Recommendation from the data: phase 1 on bwrap first, ADR before any microVM work.
2. **Computer-use scope.** Question: is desktop control limited to Verevon's sandbox VM, and is user-desktop control permanently out of scope or only parked? Items: [F-t3-024](#F-t3-024), [F-t3-026](#F-t3-026). Options: sandbox only; or sandbox plus Endpoint Plane with consent tiers. The data supports sandbox only, keeping Model Plane as proposer and a lease-bound guest executor, with every action routed through capability_policy (an unmapped tool id fails closed, so a new capability id is needed).
3. **Spaces as the chat project container, instruction precedence and multi-author authority (OD19).** Question: are Spaces the project container for chat, what is the precedence of org, Space, project and persona instructions, and whose delegated token runs a collaborator-triggered turn? Items: [F-t3-013](#F-t3-013), [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001), [F-t3-060](#F-t3-060), [F-t3-061](#F-t3-061), [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012). Options: build projects on Spaces; or add a second container (plan I21 server-synced project folders). Chat history is filtered by thread origin "chat" and Space conversations live in the Space timeline today, so making Spaces the chat project reverses a design that was kept deliberately. Recommendation: Spaces, not a second container, with the caller's Space role re-resolved server-side and never a client-supplied space_ref.
4. **Agent-browser domain policy ownership and model-blind credentials (OD13).** Question: does the broker, Control or Quarry own the domain fence and per-site read/act consent, and how does Quarry substitute vault secrets so the Model never sees them? Items: [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008), [F-t3-026](#F-t3-026), [F-t3-050](#F-t3-050), [F-t3-081](#F-t3-081). The verification data points one way: admin allow and block lists belong to Control, Space decisions may only narrow (block wins), grants are minted under the user's delegated BrowserBroker bearer and never from request JSON, and Quarry stays the final enforcer.
5. **Unattended authority for scheduled, goal and trigger runs (owner ruling 2026-09-28, OD8, D10).** Question: under what stored, expiring consent contract may unattended runs use tools and write, and how are delegated credentials renewed when the bearer lives 300 s? Items: [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018), [F-t3-037](#F-t3-037), [F-t3-047](#F-t3-047), [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002), [F-t3-019](#F-t3-019). Options: a Control-committed template or action schema carrying approved capabilities, an expiry and a ZDR refusal, re-verified at fire and per step; or a service-principal shortcut. The data rejects the shortcut: the signed decision already binds digest, expiry and ZDR refusal, so the contract belongs in Control, not a new Model Plane table.
6. **Share model, public links and artifact hosting origin under ZDR and residency.** Question: which content may be shared, by whom, on which origin, and with whose credentials do connectors run from a shared artifact? Items: [F-t3-046](#F-t3-046), [F-t3-045](#F-t3-045), [F-t3-080](#F-t3-080), [F-t3-031](#F-t3-031). Constraints from the code: ZDR threads have no durable transcript, so sharing, replay and export must refuse them; grants belong in Control's resource taxonomy, never a Model Plane-local ACL; a dedicated user-content origin is a new attack surface. Recommendation: org-only default, viewer-bound connector calls, and MCP Apps on the existing sandboxed frame before any new origin.
7. **Learned and distributed behaviour: three-tier skill library, promotion gate and plugin supply chain.** Question: what are the vendor, org and user scopes, who approves promotion, and how are executable bundles pinned and killed? Items: [F-t3-007](#F-t3-007), [F-t3-058](#F-t3-058), [F-t3-059](#F-t3-059), [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014). The loop already enables machine-written skills at org scope with no status, inbox or eval, so the recommendation is to build the staging gate (owner scope, disabled by default) before enabling the loop for real orgs; note the gateway skill cache pulls once per org per TTL and would need a service-wide view for user-scoped skills.
8. **Model entitlements and provider credential ownership.** Question: does Control Plane own allowed_models, and where do bring-your-own (BYO) keys live? Items: [F-t3-023](#F-t3-023), [F-t3-065](#F-t3-065), [F-t3-042](#F-t3-042). model-gateway refuses the OrgPolicy allowed_models field because no service owns it. CLAUDE.md:23 assigns entitlements to Control Plane, so the recommendation is Control decides, Model Plane enforces at admission (fail closed, not the fail-open org_quota behaviour at sse.rs:1154), and BYO keys stay in Integration Core custody with per-connection ZDR and residency attestation that an admin form cannot assert.
9. **LLM reviewers on the permission path (HAP WS-3, ownership-matrix rule 12).** Question: is a tighten-only model reviewer ever acceptable? Item: [F-t3-097](#F-t3-097). A standing decision rules out an LLM on the permission hot path and rule 12 says Model Plane never invents approval authority. Only a design that can move Allow to Ask or Deny, never grant, and fails closed to a human card is compatible; start in shadow mode via the hook engine if at all.
10. **Connector content in shared Spaces and ZDR propagation for connector and web reads.** Question: how is personal mailbox and drive content read in a Space thread kept from other members, and how does ZDR reach connector reads, citations and research ledgers? Items: [F-t3-010](#F-t3-010), [F-t3-056](#F-t3-056), [F-t3-027](#F-t3-027), [F-t3-055](#F-t3-055). The code shows that non-ZDR runs persist truncated tool output into step records, so connector redaction (plan F8) must land before connector reads are proven live, and web policy must be enforced in both the inline loop and execution-core's web path.

### 9.4 Dependency notes

1. **The durable loop is the trunk.** [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002) needs [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016) and the OD8 renewable bearer, and in turn gates [F-t3-011](#F-t3-011), [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018), [F-t3-037](#F-t3-037), [F-t3-047](#F-t3-047), [F-t3-026](#F-t3-026), [F-t3-036](#F-t3-036), [F-t3-064](#F-t3-064) and [F-t3-083](#F-t3-083). Verification says the loop cannot continue after an approval today and that the continuation stub resumes only provider actions, book_shipment and tickets.create; split the item so the checkpoint-and-resume core ships first.
2. **Declared tool risk before anything MCP.** [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003) precedes [F-t3-006](#F-t3-006), [F-t3-010](#F-t3-010), [F-t3-025](#F-t3-025), [F-t3-030](#F-t3-030), [F-t3-045](#F-t3-045), [F-t3-057](#F-t3-057), [F-t3-059](#F-t3-059) and [F-t3-070](#F-t3-070), because both loops refuse MCP execution until capability-core provides a durable per-tool binding and the binding may never be synthesised from a model-supplied name.
3. **Context before cache before personalisation.** [F-t3-022](#F-t3-022) and [F-t3-020](#F-t3-020) feed [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001); [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001) feeds [F-t3-013](#F-t3-013), [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018), [F-t3-060](#F-t3-060) and [F-t3-061](#F-t3-061). Do runs restart from preamble plus goal today, so cross-run cache reuse is only the preamble and tools until [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001) lands.
4. **The computer track is strictly ordered.** [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008) then [F-t3-026](#F-t3-026); [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009) then [F-t3-035](#F-t3-035) then [F-t3-024](#F-t3-024); [F-t3-043](#F-t3-043), [F-t3-084](#F-t3-084), [F-t3-085](#F-t3-085) and [F-t3-087](#F-t3-087) all wait for [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009). Nearly all of the XL cost in [F-t3-024](#F-t3-024) sits in [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009) and must not be counted twice.
5. **The data-model track.** [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012) precedes [F-t3-013](#F-t3-013), [F-t3-015](#F-t3-015), [F-t3-031](#F-t3-031), [F-t3-046](#F-t3-046), [F-t3-052](#F-t3-052), [F-t3-069](#F-t3-069) and [F-t3-077](#F-t3-077). The full-text index must live in session-core (no cross-plane DB), exclude ZDR turns and honour the erase contract; the persisted message tree should follow the index, not lead it.
6. **Governance surfaces chain.** [F-t3-032](#F-t3-032) feeds [F-t3-043](#F-t3-043), [F-t3-055](#F-t3-055), [F-t3-059](#F-t3-059), [F-t3-063](#F-t3-063) and [F-t3-074](#F-t3-074); policy may only tighten relative to the server floor.
7. **Model record before provider breadth.** [F-t3-023](#F-t3-023) feeds [F-t3-042](#F-t3-042), which feeds [F-t3-028](#F-t3-028), [F-t3-040](#F-t3-040), [F-t3-041](#F-t3-041), [F-t3-065](#F-t3-065), [F-t3-072](#F-t3-072), [F-t3-073](#F-t3-073) and [F-t3-095](#F-t3-095); [F-t3-041](#F-t3-041) also needs [F-t3-029](#F-t3-029) and a Vertex attestation, and in turn gates [F-t3-051](#F-t3-051) and [F-t3-090](#F-t3-090).
8. **Attachments and Office.** [F-t3-017](#F-t3-017) precedes [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014), [F-t3-051](#F-t3-051), [F-t3-052](#F-t3-052), [F-t3-090](#F-t3-090) and [F-t3-091](#F-t3-091); [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014) precedes [F-t3-038](#F-t3-038) and [F-t3-058](#F-t3-058).
9. **Deploy before you build on it.** [F-t3-039](#F-t3-039) is a dependency of [F-t3-004](#F-t3-004) and [F-t3-079](#F-t3-079); [F-t3-005](#F-t3-005) and [F-t3-004](#F-t3-004) are prerequisites of [F-t3-062](#F-t3-062) and [F-t3-068](#F-t3-068).
10. **Demoted items should still be slotted.** [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016) was demoted because drain and Redis resume already ship and approvals already persist, but it remains a listed dependency of [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002), [F-t3-011](#F-t3-011), [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012), [F-t3-015](#F-t3-015) and [F-t3-064](#F-t3-064); treat its remaining M slice (shared cancel and queue state) as W1 work.

## 10. Risks and open questions

### 10.1 Main risks

1. **Plane-boundary erosion.** A thread-scoped sandbox workspace, staged attachments and connector content in Spaces all tempt shortcuts across planes. Verification found concrete cases: the audit hash chain must be delivered through the outbox and NATS and never touch audit-core's database ([F-t3-004](#F-t3-004)); the full-text index must sit in session-core, not Data Plane v2 ([F-t3-012](#F-t3-012)); grants for snapshots belong in Control's resource taxonomy ([F-t3-046](#F-t3-046)); no single Model Plane resolver can see every policy layer, so each owner plane must expose its own value and origin ([F-t3-068](#F-t3-068)). Also [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009), [F-t3-017](#F-t3-017), [F-t3-010](#F-t3-010), [F-t3-057](#F-t3-057).
2. **ZDR propagation into new stores.** Loop checkpoints, the search index, artifact versions, request capture, recordings, push payloads, terminal output, goal rows and telemetry exports are new places content could persist. The code already refuses several of these under ZDR (continuation, durable upload, artifacts, handles), so each item must carry an explicit ZDR clause and the resolver's golden matrix should gain a case per store. Items: [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002), [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012), [F-t3-015](#F-t3-015), [F-t3-031](#F-t3-031), [F-t3-034](#F-t3-034), [F-t3-035](#F-t3-035), [F-t3-037](#F-t3-037), [F-t3-046](#F-t3-046), [F-t3-050](#F-t3-050), [F-t3-071](#F-t3-071), [F-t3-075](#F-t3-075). Notification-core's README says the full payload still reaches Novu, so attention notifications must be content-free on all threads ([F-t3-015](#F-t3-015)).
3. **Authority creep.** Scoped approvals, stored consent for unattended runs, takeover carry-over, persistent named agents, viewer-bound artifact connectors and hooks each widen who or what can act. They must be tighten-by-default, expiring and audited; the Watch primitive deliberately refuses org-wide subscriptions and a stored record is not authority ([F-t3-047](#F-t3-047)). Items: [F-t3-006](#F-t3-006), [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018), [F-t3-019](#F-t3-019), [F-t3-025](#F-t3-025), [F-t3-032](#F-t3-032), [F-t3-045](#F-t3-045), [F-t3-063](#F-t3-063).
4. **A larger prompt-injection surface.** Browser pages, mail bodies, recall snippets, MCP tools, widgets, teach-mode recordings and imported memory feed untrusted text into the loop. The semantic screening pass is chat-only and shadow-only today, and two scanner copies are kept in sync by hand ([F-t3-033](#F-t3-033)). [F-t3-033](#F-t3-033) should land before or with [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008), [F-t3-010](#F-t3-010), [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003), [F-t3-044](#F-t3-044) and [F-t3-054](#F-t3-054).
5. **Cost growth without measurement.** More context per Do run, two-pass research, wide fan-out, image loops and VMs raise spend while the cache hit rate is unmeasured, image generation is not in the cost ledger and Gemini turns would be unmetered without pricing. [F-t3-022](#F-t3-022) and [F-t3-028](#F-t3-028) come first; see also [F-t3-036](#F-t3-036), [F-t3-037](#F-t3-037), [F-t3-041](#F-t3-041), [F-t3-051](#F-t3-051), [F-t3-092](#F-t3-092).
6. **Concentrated dependencies.** A third of the roadmap hangs on [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002), [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003) and [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009), and the renewable delegated credential (OD8) is a hidden prerequisite for [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002), [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018), [F-t3-036](#F-t3-036) and [F-t3-037](#F-t3-037). A slip there delays everything downstream.
7. **Effort was systematically underestimated.** Verification changed 40 effort estimates, 30 of them upward (15 from L to XL; the 11 in Priority and Recommended are [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008), [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014), [F-t3-027](#F-t3-027), [F-t3-032](#F-t3-032), [F-t3-037](#F-t3-037), [F-t3-043](#F-t3-043), [F-t3-046](#F-t3-046), [F-t3-047](#F-t3-047), [F-t3-051](#F-t3-051), [F-t3-058](#F-t3-058), [F-t3-062](#F-t3-062)) mostly because items bundle separable deliverables. Plan each XL as the split the verification proposes, not as one epic.
8. **Operational maturity.** The audit trail went undelivered with a fix built but not deployed, the ZDR unblock waits on an operator and commercial attestation, and no signed release candidate or rehearsed rollback exists. The evidence register for release tooling is dated 2026-08-17 and the conformance script reads a markdown table row, so state must be re-verified live ([F-t3-039](#F-t3-039), [F-t3-004](#F-t3-004), [F-t3-005](#F-t3-005)).
9. **Design conflicts inside the repo.** Several items contradict a documented ruling: queued-input classification is deliberately left to the model ([F-t3-016](#F-t3-016)); persisted message versions were rejected once ([F-t3-012](#F-t3-012)); the SandboxBackend trait was rejected until a second backend exists ([F-t3-009](#F-t3-009)); subscription fallback clashes with ownership-matrix rule 17 ([F-t3-072](#F-t3-072)); the @ trigger is reserved for Space-governed agent mentions ([F-t3-052](#F-t3-052)). Each needs an explicit reversal, not a silent change.
10. **Supply chain for executable extensions.** SKILL.md bundles, plugins and per-Space images bring tenant-authored and third-party code into the sandbox. Bundle scripts may run only inside the sandbox bound to the low-risk cap.command.sandbox, stdio and binary plugin components must be refused and reported, and install must list every component ([F-t3-014](#F-t3-014), [F-t3-059](#F-t3-059), [F-t3-084](#F-t3-084)).

### 10.2 Open questions

1. OD19: are Spaces the chat project container, what is the instruction precedence (org, Space, project, persona), what are the branch and rewind semantics, what is the share model, and who may author slash templates? ([F-t3-013](#F-t3-013), [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001), [F-t3-012](#F-t3-012), [F-t3-046](#F-t3-046), [F-t3-052](#F-t3-052))
2. OD13: who owns the agent-browser domain fence and per-site read/act consent: broker, Control or Quarry? ([F-t3-008](#F-t3-008))
3. OD8: how are delegated credentials renewed for runs that pause for hours or start unattended, without a service-principal shortcut? ([F-t3-002](#F-t3-002), [F-t3-018](#F-t3-018), [F-t3-037](#F-t3-037))
4. Owner ruling 2026-09-28: under what stored-consent contract may scheduled and triggered runs perform writes, and does the contract live in the Control-committed template as verification suggests? ([F-t3-018](#F-t3-018), [F-t3-047](#F-t3-047))
5. OD6: how does a plan continue after an approval pause: loop-level checkpoints (as the references do) or more action kinds in the continuation worker? ([F-t3-002](#F-t3-002), [F-t3-011](#F-t3-011))
6. Learning: what are the three skill tiers, should learned skills auto-accept at user scope once an eval passes, and which runs are reviewed? ([F-t3-007](#F-t3-007), [F-t3-058](#F-t3-058))
7. ZDR: which provider surface is attested first, Azure OpenAI or Azure AI Foundry, and when does Z-40 close commercially? Verification notes the two need separate attestations and that the Z-60 live proof runs first on a ZDR-TEST org with nothing attested, so a real-provider pass is a second step. ([F-t3-005](#F-t3-005))
8. Sandbox computer: self-hosted microVMs or an attested managed provider, which region and cost model, and are workspaces scoped to the thread, the Space or both? ([F-t3-009](#F-t3-009))
9. Computer use: is user-desktop control permanently out of scope or only parked? This decides whether the Endpoint Plane ever joins the agent path. ([F-t3-024](#F-t3-024))
10. OD7: what spend threshold triggers a confirm gate for wide research and other fan-out, given the plan's "no confirm gate in v1"? ([F-t3-036](#F-t3-036))
11. F12: should Ask mode read connected apps, as the references do, or stay Do-only? Connector reads in non-ZDR runs persist truncated output into step records, so the answer depends on connector redaction landing first. ([F-t3-010](#F-t3-010))
12. OD2: when is Do mode enabled on the product route? File tools, MCP and connectors in Do depend on it. ([F-t3-030](#F-t3-030), [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003))
13. Gemini: can Vertex AI in an EU region meet the residency and ZDR attestation bar? ([F-t3-041](#F-t3-041), [F-t3-051](#F-t3-051), [F-t3-090](#F-t3-090))
14. Entitlements and BYO: does Control Plane own allowed_models, and how are customer-billed BYO connections metered and attested? ([F-t3-023](#F-t3-023), [F-t3-065](#F-t3-065))
15. Effort in Do runs: the agent loop deliberately disables thinking (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:1336-1339), so extending the effort ladder to Do runs is a product decision, not a bug fix. ([F-t3-029](#F-t3-029))
16. Channel Plane: when does it move from docs-only to build? Outward channel adapters and channel-origin runs through the Agent API wait on it. ([F-t3-089](#F-t3-089), [F-t3-064](#F-t3-064))
17. HAP WS-3 and rule 12: is a tighten-only LLM reviewer ever acceptable on the permission path? ([F-t3-097](#F-t3-097))

### 10.3 Evidence limits that matter for decisions

- The four web products (claude.ai, ChatGPT, Perplexity, manus.im) rest on public documentation only [DOC]. Where a best practice comes solely from them, such as the per-conversation VM behind [F-t3-009](#F-t3-009), model entitlements behind [F-t3-023](#F-t3-023), memory controls behind [F-t3-020](#F-t3-020), deep research with an editable plan behind [F-t3-027](#F-t3-027) and the Compliance API behind [F-t3-004](#F-t3-004), the mechanism is not verified and only the product shape should be copied.
- Claude desktop's instruction scopes come from i18n label strings, not runtime assembly code, and the only documented precedence is that organisation instructions override personal preferences; the "org > Space > persona > user" ordering is Verevon's proposed design and must not be attributed to a reference [REA, corrected]. The reference check for [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001) also marks "one assembler used by chat and agents" and "every reference agent run inherits the conversation" as inference [INF].
- Fourteen items had a wrong prevalence count before correction; where the corrected count is not stated, this document avoids "n of N references" claims. The data-connected dashboard half of [F-t3-091](#F-t3-091) rests on one of twelve sources and documentation only [DOC].
- Several Verevon "built" states cannot be proven from code: whether the audit outbox fix is deployed ([F-t3-004](#F-t3-004)), whether the signed release candidate exists ([F-t3-039](#F-t3-039), register dated 2026-08-17), and whether Dragonfly or Redis is present in every deployment ([F-t3-016](#F-t3-016)). These are operator facts and should be checked live before planning.
- Some mechanism claims are inference from naming or field lists rather than observed behaviour: Cursor's "hydration flags" on RequestContext [INF], the SPA's browser-local transcript snapshot inferred from history.rs naming ([F-t3-021](#F-t3-021)) [INF], and the assumption that Responses continuation applies only to platform OpenAI or Azure routes ([F-t3-095](#F-t3-095)) [INF].
- Demand evidence is thin for the sandbox-extension items ([F-t3-084](#F-t3-084), [F-t3-085](#F-t3-085)) and for code-edit quality ([F-t3-088](#F-t3-088)); they were demoted on that basis and should not be scheduled without a concrete user need.

## Appendix A. Method, sources and limits

### A.1 What was analysed

Twelve reference sources were analysed: six installed desktop apps, two open-source repositories and four public web products. Verevon itself was read at CoresSystem commit afb83928 (2026-10-10). The table gives each source's version, kind, analysis method and the number of structured feature records it produced.

| Source | Version or commit | Kind | How it was analysed | Feature records |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Claude desktop app | 2.31226.1 | installed app | REA on app.asar plus the extracted bundle | 449 |
| Hermes Agent | commit b23600860f (2026-10-12) | open-source repo | source and docs | 278 |
| DeepSeek harness | commit d743267388 (2026-10-09) | open-source repo | source and docs | 335 |
| T3 Code | 0.0.35 | installed app | REA on app.asar plus recovered sources | 203 |
| Manus (Manus Studio) | 2.0.6 | installed app | REA on app.asar (targeted subdirectories) plus the extracted bundle | 218 |
| Codex desktop app (plugins, agent runtime, models) | 26.1007.2314.0 | installed app | REA on app.asar and .vite build plus native binary strings | 227 |
| Google Antigravity (agent hub) | 2.11.0 | installed app | REA plus direct reading of the embedded UI and protobuf descriptors | 247 |
| Cursor | 3.17.21 | installed app | REA on extension bundles plus direct reading | 287 |
| manus.im | public docs, October 2026 | public documentation | public web docs only (DOC) | 105 |
| perplexity.ai | public docs, October 2026 | public documentation | public web docs only (DOC) | 112 |
| chatgpt.com | public docs, October 2026 | public documentation | public web docs only (DOC) | 155 |
| claude.ai | public docs, October 2026 | public documentation | public web docs only (DOC) | 127 |

### A.2 The process

The audit ran in seven steps.

1. **Inventory.** Each source was inventoried once: install layout, manifests, bundles, binaries, documentation and, for the web products, the public help and blog pages.
2. **Deep dives.** Each installed app and repository was split into two facets (for example product UX and runtime architecture for Claude). Each facet produced a narrative and a JSON file of feature records; every record carries its category, evidence (file, line or page), provenance tag and quality rating. The deep dives wrote their own limitations sections, which A.3 and A.4 draw on.
3. **Code-verified Verevon baseline.** Three baselines (Model Plane, chat and agents UI, planned work and decisions) were built by reading the CoresSystem code and decision ledgers. Each of the 500 records states shipped, partial, absent, stub or planned, with a code path. Because many repository documents are stale, code was treated as ground truth and documents as intent.
4. **Cross-source comparison.** The 2,743 reference records were grouped into 595 capability clusters across 13 categories. Each cluster records prevalence, the best implementation and the Verevon status.
5. **Consolidation.** Each category's clusters became recommendations (159, R-t3-*) with a gap statement, the reference best practice, an approach and an effort estimate.
6. **Tier calibration.** One pass merged duplicates across categories (the same defect often surfaced in several), assigned tier, rank and wave, and produced the 98 final items (F-t3-*); two items were dropped.
7. **Two independent verification passes.** A Verevon-side pass read the code for every item and rewrote status, exact gap, evidence paths, reusable parts, constraints, approach and effort. A reference-side pass re-opened the cited evidence for every Priority item and tried to refute it, correcting over-reach and prevalence counts. Verified data supersedes the earlier drafts throughout.

Model tiers by job: inventories ran on Claude Haiku 5.5 at maximum effort; deep dives on Claude Sonnet 5.5 at extra-high effort; the baseline and the cross-source comparison on Claude Opus 5.5 at high effort; web research and document writing on Claude Fable 5.1 at medium effort.

### A.3 How REA was used, and its limits

REA (the reverse-engineering analysis toolset) was run only on installed apps and only against read-only copies: extracted app.asar archives, Vite or Electron build directories and PE executables. It was not used on Hermes or DeepSeek, which are source repositories [SRC]. Its JavaScript analysis (`analyze_javascript_application`) produced module graphs, IPC inventories and findings, and `inspect_pe_resources` confirmed integrity resources, version data and the absence of symbols on native helpers [REA].

Its limits were consistent across the apps. On large minified bundles the semantic pass hit the 2 GB heap: Antigravity's main.js exhausted the heap and left the graph partial [REA]; Claude's whole-app run exhausted the semantic budget, so the deep dive analysed a targeted subset and left out the 6.9 MB core chunk [REA]; Codex's code-review plugin ran out of heap on two files [REA]; Manus coverage was reported partial with 355 limitations [REA]. String-seed tracing (`trace_application_feature`) does not index string literals in these minified graphs, so seeds that `grep` found directly returned no match for Claude, T3 Code and Antigravity; the tools state that this is not evidence of absence [REA]. As a result, most app evidence in this audit is tagged SRC: the deep dives read the extracted or beautified bundles directly and used REA for structure. Native binaries were examined through PE resources and string extraction only; the Codex Rust runtime, the Manus Go and Rust helpers and the Cursor sandbox helper were never decompiled or run, so claims about their behaviour rest on names, messages and prompt text [SRC, INF]. The Claude copy is the Windows MSIX build, so the macOS and Linux variants (Swift helper, bubblewrap) were not available [SRC]. Cursor had no pre-extracted copy; the analysed files were a scratch copy of its `out/` directory [SRC].

### A.4 Limits that affect confidence

- **Server-side behaviour is invisible.** Every installed app is a client. Manus's cloud side (agent, sandbox VM, policy engines) is absent from the bundle; Cursor's models, routing and review services were not observable; Codex's sandbox, approval and model logic sits in the Rust runtime. Statements about what these servers do are DOC or INF [SRC].
- **Web products are public-docs-only.** claude.ai, chatgpt.com, perplexity.ai and manus.im were read from help pages, blogs and press. Their claims are DOC and were not exercised; one page could not be re-fetched during verification (HTTP 403).
- **Nothing was executed or launched.** No app, installer, project code, test, build or dev server was run; no user data, cookie or credential store was read. Runtime-only behaviour (feature flags, server-pushed configuration, consent flows) is invisible; several Cursor and Claude mechanisms are flag-gated with server-controlled values and are reported as such.
- **Minified code reads are lossy.** UI behaviour in Manus and T3 Code was partly reconstructed from i18n keys; Claude's instruction-scope list comes from label strings, not assembly code.
- **Verevon documents are partly stale.** One baseline (connector reads) was corrected by a re-read on 2026-10-14. Verified item data wins over the planning documents quoted in cards.
- **Reference code evolves.** Repositories and apps were read at the commits and versions above; later releases may close or open gaps.
- **Prevalence counts are approximate.** Verification found 14 wrong counts among the Priority items; corrected counts are used where available and omitted otherwise.

### A.5 Counts

- Reference feature records: 2,743 across 12 sources.
- Verevon baseline records: 500 across 13 categories (290 shipped, 122 partial, 57 absent, 9 stub, 22 planned).
- Capability clusters compared: 595 in 13 categories.
- Consolidated recommendations: 159 (R-t3-*), merged into 98 final items (F-t3-*), 2 dropped.
- Tiers: 23 Priority, 45 Recommended, 30 Nice-to-have before verification; 22, 46 and 30 after.
- Verevon-side verification: 98 items read; verdicts keep 13, reword 81, demote 4; effort revised on 40; one tier change; 0 items still unverified, 0 low-confidence.
- Reference-side verification: 23 Priority items re-checked; 2 confirmed, 21 partially confirmed with corrections; 14 prevalence counts corrected.
- Verified Verevon status of the 98 items: 78 Partial, 16 Absent, 2 Stub, 2 Planned.

## Appendix C. Reference-side verification log (Priority items)

Every Priority recommendation was re-checked by an independent skeptic that re-opened the cited evidence in the reference apps, repos or public docs and tried to refute it. This log records what that check corrected. The cards in section 4 already incorporate these corrections.

### [F-t3-001](#F-t3-001): One server-assembled run context for Do, Plan and scheduled runs: instruction stack, thread history, persona, memory and run notes
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence medium).

**Corrections:** (1) Claude does have seven instruction-scope labels (Global, Folder, Space, Project, Channel, Profile, Organization), but the only documented precedence is 'organization takes priority over users' personal preferences'. Ordering among the other scopes is not documented. (2) The character limits are generic 'shorten to {max} characters' messages; the per-scope budget values were not observed, except organizationInstructions max 3000 (SRC). (3) 'One assembler used by chat and agents' is inference from shared labels; no code path was observed. (4) The ordering 'org > Space/project > agent persona > user' is Verevon's proposed design and should not be attributed to a reference. (5) Cursor's activation modes are confirmed in source (always/glob/agent-fetched/manual). 'Hydration flags' on RequestContext rests on field lists, and I did not re-verify them. (6) 'Every reference agent run inherits the conversation' is INF.

**Caveats:** ChatGPT, Claude.ai, Manus and Perplexity layered-instruction support is DOC-only; Claude scope list is from i18n labels (strings), not runtime assembly code; Automation memory in Cursor is SRC/medium from service names; sand_memory_dreaming behaviour unobserved

### [F-t3-002](#F-t3-002): Durable agent loop: checkpoint every round and pause, resume in place after approval, answer or crash, close dangling tool calls, effect ledger
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** 1) 'Pause, resume in place after approval' as a crash-durable continuation is not shown by any reference. Hermes blocks the agent thread on an in-memory threading.Event with a deadline, and replays open requests only to reconnecting clients while the process lives. DeepSeek requests are valid only inside an open turn, and its README defers a durable out-of-turn approval workflow. Present durable approval pauses as a Verevon design synthesis (INF) built from DeepSeek checkpoints, not as a reference behavior. 2) The Manus 'exactly-once ledger' is a process-local, in-memory Map (max 4096 entries, 10-minute horizon) scoped to browser MCP side-effect actions only. It deduplicates retries within a live process; it is not a durable cross-crash ledger. Mismatched digests return a replay-conflict result (confirmed). 3) DeepSeek explicitly disclaims exactly-once: it records dispatch intent, writes an unknown-outcome closer instead of retrying, and recommends forwarding exec.callId as an idempotency key. It has no checkpoint inside an active model attempt. 4) Cursor auto-continue and durable question delivery are server-flag-gated (glass_auto_continue_*, ask_question_durable_delivery fallbac…

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

**Caveats:** Manus ledger: browser-runtime only, in-memory (SRC).; Cursor recovery behavior is flag-gated with server-controlled values (SRC strings/flags only).; Claude desktop auto-resume (claude-product-ux-087) is medium confidence and was not exercised at runtime.; Durable approval pause and resume across crashes is INF (synthesis), not observed in any reference.

### [F-t3-003](#F-t3-003): Declared tool side-effect classes, a never-auto set and per-tool allow/ask/never policy, so MCP tools become callable through the signed gate
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** (1) Manus is over-reached. The five-level side-effect map covers only its 22 browser MCP tools. In code it drives only the replay/idempotency action ledger (hasExternalSideEffect -> action key plus digest dedupe) and the MCP annotations. It does not drive approvals or policy. Making 'declared side-effect class drives approval policy' the reference practice is a Verevon synthesis (INF). The closest real references are Hermes (a binary readOnlyHint check, only on servers marked untrusted, asked once per call with no stored allow/ask/never), Claude (a per-tool toolPolicy for its two built-in connectors, with code-defined ask defaults and readOnlyHint skipping) and ChatGPT (DOC, opaque risk tiers). (2) Hermes has a per-SERVER trust tier, not a per-tool allow/ask/never store. (3) In Perplexity, the 'Always allow / Always ask / Never allow' labels appear only in image alt text; the body copy describes Always ask plus once / thread / deny, and recurring runs inheriting thread grants. (4) Claude's never-allow set is 8 tools per BUILT-IN connector (GitHub and Microsoft 365 only), not a general MCP feature. The 'static never-auto set, mode-rank ratchet' is a separate Claude mechanism (claud…

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

### [F-t3-004](#F-t3-004): Deliver the AI-action audit trail, then make it tamper-evident with a compliance query API and SIEM export
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** (1) Claude's HMAC-chained audit.jsonl is a LOCAL per-session Cowork log in the desktop app. Its key sits beside the log under the same OS user, so it detects naive or partial edits, not a same-user attacker, and it falls back to unsigned entries when safeStorage is unavailable. It is not a server-side compliance store and must not be presented as the 'tamper-evident compliance' reference. No reference was verified to hash-chain a hosted audit store. (2) No inspected source combines hash-chaining with a compliance query API and SIEM export. That combination is a Verevon synthesis. (3) Claude.ai enterprise audit logs list no agent/AI-action events, so the cluster text 'AI actions incl. browser actions' comes from Manus' Browser Operator doc, not from Claude. (4) Prevalence: DeepSeek is counted in C-11-047 although it is a counter-example (approval events without actor identity), and Perplexity record 102 (Comet browser telemetry) is not an AI-action audit log. Cursor's action audit log is a team-admin setting with client flag default off, plus telemetry. The real count is closer to 5 vendors (Anthropic, OpenAI, Perplexity, Manus, Cursor-flagged), and the two clusters should be merge…

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

**Caveats:** All hosted audit/compliance features are enterprise-only (Claude Enterprise; ChatGPT Enterprise/Edu; Perplexity 50+ seats or Enterprise Max; Manus compliance API gated by customer success); ChatGPT logs exclude agent-mode internal actions (per record notes); Cursor action audit logs flag-gated (client default off); Claude HMAC chain is local and same-user-forgeable; the unsigned fallback exists; ChatGPT claims DOC-only and not re-verified (HTTP 403)

Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

Cursor has no account memory store: legacy memory RPCs are probably retired, and current learning is transcript search plus per-automation memories. Use 8 of 12 at most (7 distinct vendors). 2) A separate pause control (keep rows, stop use and generation) is shown only for Claude. Perplexity's doc has toggles but no distinct pause. ChatGPT pause applies to Computer History, not memory. Do not imply pause is table-stakes. 3) Org governance '3 of 12' counts Claude desktop and claude.ai separately: 2 distinct vendors. Both confirmed: Perplexity Enterprise admins manage memory org-wide and memories belong to the org; Claude Team/Enterprise owners enable memory and members opt in. Turning it off at org level deletes all memory data. 4) The topic-based-entries claim is correct for the current claude.ai experience (doc). The nightly summary still exists in desktop strings and is the legacy mode. 5) claude.ai memory i…

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

**Caveats:** Perplexity org controls and memory ownership: DOC-only (Enterprise).; Claude desktop memory UI: SRC strings. Both the legacy nightly-summary mode and the topic-entry mode are present in the shipped strings.; Codex memories are config-driven (generate_memories/use_memories) and may be feature-gated.; claude.ai memory is excluded for custom-retention, HIPAA and public-sector orgs (DOC).

### [F-t3-021](#F-t3-021): Transcript performance: benchmark first, then block-level streaming render, a virtualised timeline and paged transcript reads
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** Wrong lead source: Claude's 257-module tiered lazy registry is about code-splitting and boot. It is not evidence of transcript render performance. For the transcript, the leads are T3 Code (virtualised timeline, SRC), DeepSeek (block freeze and resumable highlighting) and Claude desktop (tail-first and paged transcript reads over IPC and MessagePort, SRC). Keep Claude's registry only as a startup/chunk-resilience sub-point. Raise DeepSeek's provenance from DOC to SRC: the freeze-trailing-2-blocks, grammar-state resume and 1000-char no-colour rules are in incremental.ts and highlight.ts. The DeepSeek startup budget test counts only settings/describe calls (budget 2), not all /api calls, so do not describe it as a general cold-boot request budget. No reference was found that benchmarks transcript rendering, so 'benchmark first' is a Verevon recommendation, not a reference practice.

**Caveats:** ChatGPT paged loading is DOC (release note); Antigravity virtualisation evidence is a TanStack Virtual polyfill note and a diff worker (medium confidence); Claude memory-pressure eviction is inferred from analytics event names only; REA was not re-run; installed Claude chunks were read directly with node

### [F-t3-022](#F-t3-022): Prompt-cache discipline: measured hit rate, stable/context/volatile prompt tiers with an append-only tail, lineage-scoped cache keys and compaction transactions
Reference-side check: **confirmed** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** Minor: 'measured hit rate' is a Verevon recommendation, not a reference practice. References record cache read and write tokens (Cursor, Antigravity, DeepSeek), and Hermes counts OpenRouter cache HIT headers. None exposes a hit-rate KPI, and DeepSeek's README says measured hit rates are not guaranteed.

**Caveats:** Manus KV-cache lessons and todo recitation are DOC (blog), not product code; web-claudeai prompt-caching record is a usage-limit help article (weak DOC); hermes-architecture-and-surfaces-018 and -014 are DOC-tagged; the SRC records (-056, -112, -016, -072) carry the claim

### [F-t3-023](#F-t3-023): Org and role model entitlements: Control Plane decides, Model Plane enforces at admission
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence medium).

**Corrections:** (1) Prevalence is overstated. t3code-ui-and-multimodel-090 gates models by the version of the provider's CLI. It has no plan, role or org entitlement, so t3code should not be counted. claude-product-ux-139 is a staff-only tier-simulation switch. Real count: 4 of 12 (claude, codexagent, web-chatgpt, web-claudeai), 'common' rather than table-stakes. (2) 'New models are off by default for enterprise' is per-launch wording for named models (Astra, Sol, Luna in Enterprise/Edu). It is not a standing rule, so phrase it as 'recent models launched off by default'. (3) Per-surface defaults come from the Work & Codex articles (20001275, 11369540), not from the two URLs cited on the record, so fix the citation. (4) 'Additive roles' is right for ChatGPT model access (a role that allows access keeps it enabled even if another role turns it off). Note that ChatGPT also has Lockdown roles that override, per the audit's own deep notes. (5) The Codex part is plan-list gating in the model catalogue data, not org-role policy.

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

**Caveats:** ChatGPT part is DOC-only, and the live help pages were unreachable (403), so it was verified against cached copies; Claude role and effort caps are SRC from UI strings only; the enforcement path was not traced; Model Test and the role controls are Enterprise/Edu admin features (plan-gated); Codex catalogue was carved from the codex.exe binary by an earlier agent; not re-carved here

## Appendix D. What code verification changed

Code verification read the Verevon implementation for every recommendation. Outcome: 98 recommendations kept in the list, 1 moved down one tier, 0 dropped as already built; 81 were reworded because the code showed a more precise gap, a better place to build it, or reusable parts.

### Tier changes after code verification

- [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016) Multi-replica-safe run control: shared cancel, queued-input, plan-mode and turn state, durable send receipts and an editable message queue: Priority to Recommended. Real but a scale-readiness hardening for a single-replica deployment with drain and Redis resume already shipped, and it is not a precondition for durable runs because approvals already persist in session-core; the queue UX part needs a design decision first.

### Effort estimates changed by verification

- [F-t3-004](#F-t3-004) Deliver the AI-action audit trail, then make it tamper-evident with a compliance query API and SIEM export: M to L. Raised from M: Phase 0 is S, but the chain with retention checkpoints and multi-bus ordering plus the compliance API and fan-out is M to L.
- [F-t3-005](#F-t3-005) Unblock Zero Data Retention: attest one provider and run the live proof: S to M. Attestation itself is S (set env fields and digest), but collecting contract evidence, deploying uncommitted multi-wave work in order, fixing Z-41b findings and doing two live passes make the unblock M.
- [F-t3-008](#F-t3-008) Agent browser in chat and Do mode on real broker grants, with per-site read/act consent, admin allow/block lists and model-blind logins: L to XL. As bundled (grant issuer, broker persistence, Do-mode browser subagent, Control-owned site policy, vault substitution, state diffs) it sums E2 M + E4 M + E9 M + E10 L + E12 L plus net-new governance and vault work; split into grant plus Do tool (L) and site governance (L).
- [F-t3-014](#F-t3-014) Verified Office deliverables through executable SKILL.md bundles: docx, xlsx, pptx and pdf skills with structural checks, render-and-look and an in-panel previewer: L to XL. As written (bundle format, sandbox mount, LibreOffice image, four first-party skills with checkers, previewer registry, supply-chain rules) is XL; split per the adjustment it becomes roughly L + M + M.
- [F-t3-016](#F-t3-016) Multi-replica-safe run control: shared cancel, queued-input, plan-mode and turn state, durable send receipts and an editable message queue: L to M. Reduced from L: the Redis buffer, drain and durable receipts are already in place and the queue UX conflicts with the current design; the remaining shared cancel and queue state is M.
- [F-t3-020](#F-t3-020) User and org memory controls: on, pause, reset and org policy: M to L. M for a user-level on/paused/off switch plus Reset (session-core state column, Dreaming and recall gates, gateway route, MemorySection controls). Org policy, learning-review gating and the Ask-mode remember path make the full item L.
- [F-t3-023](#F-t3-023) Org and role model entitlements: Control Plane decides, Model Plane enforces at admission: M to L. M for an org-level allow/deny list with default model and ListModels filter using the org_quota pull pattern; role/group/plan resolution, effort cap and the explain endpoint make the full item L.
- [F-t3-026](#F-t3-026) Human takeover and hand-back from live views: agent-requested takeover for logins, 2FA and CAPTCHAs, then point-and-comment steering: L to M. File-level only is L; hunk-level adds M.

## Appendix E. Glossary

### Verevon terms

- **ADR (Architecture Decision Record).** A short record of one architecture decision and its trade-offs. "ADR needed" means leadership must decide before the affected items are built.
- **Plane.** A service group in the CoresSystem monorepo that owns one domain. Control Plane: identity, users, organisations, billing, sessions, audit, quotas, entitlements. Data Plane v2: documents, chunks, embeddings, retrieval, graph, wiki. Ingestion Plane: captures evidence and persists durable knowledge through Data Plane contracts (it holds Quarry v2 and integration-corev2). Model Plane: reasoning, sessions and runs, inference, execution loops, capabilities, sandboxes, browser grants, cost. Application Plane: collaborative and realtime workspace projections and notifications. Frontend Plane: the Verevon v3 user interface and the same-origin gateway. Support Plane: the RustDesk-compatible remote-support rendezvous and relay. Channel Plane: future, documentation only. Planes do not read each other's databases.
- **Space.** Verevon's governed room shared by people and agents.
- **Quarry (Quarry v2).** Verevon's web acquisition engine in the Ingestion Plane: crawl, scrape, extract and browser automation. The Model Plane proposes browser actions; Quarry executes or rejects them.
- **BrowserBroker.** The Model Plane service that issues browser grants, which are the permission for one run to act in a browser.
- **integration-corev2 (IC).** The Ingestion Plane service that executes provider actions and connector calls.
- **ZDR (Zero Data Retention).** A setting under which content must not be persisted or logged. In Verevon it has to hold in every plane that could store content.
- **W1 to W5.** The implementation waves in section 9.
- **OD n, D n, H n, E n, I n, S n.m.** Identifiers of open decisions, decisions and plan rows in Verevon's own planning documents (for example "open decision OD19"). They are kept as written so the team can look them up.

### General terms

- **ACL.** Access control list: the rules saying who may do what to a resource.
- **API / SDK / CLI.** Application programming interface; software development kit; command-line interface.
- **AX (accessibility tree).** The structured description of a page that screen readers use; browser agents read it to find elements.
- **BFF.** Backend for frontend: a server layer that serves one frontend and calls the other services on its behalf.
- **BYO.** Bring your own (for example your own keys or models).
- **C2PA.** A standard for attaching provenance information to generated media.
- **CAPTCHA.** A challenge meant to tell humans from bots.
- **CAS.** Content-addressed storage: files are stored and found by a hash of their content.
- **CDP.** Chrome DevTools Protocol: the remote-control interface of Chromium browsers.
- **CEL.** Common Expression Language: a small language for writing policy conditions.
- **CI.** Continuous integration (automated build and test runs).
- **CSP.** Content Security Policy: a browser header that limits what a page may load or run.
- **DLP.** Data loss prevention.
- **DM.** Direct message.
- **DND.** Do not disturb.
- **DOM.** Document Object Model: the live tree of a web page.
- **DOCX / OOXML.** Microsoft Office's zipped XML document formats.
- **GDPR / HIPAA.** European and United States data-protection laws.
- **HITL.** Human in the loop: a person approves or steers an agent step.
- **HMAC.** A keyed hash used to sign data so tampering is detectable.
- **IDE.** Integrated development environment (a code editor).
- **IPC.** Inter-process communication.
- **JSONL.** JSON Lines: one JSON object per line.
- **JWT.** JSON Web Token: a signed token that carries claims.
- **LLM.** Large language model.
- **MCP.** Model Context Protocol: an open protocol for giving agents tools and data.
- **MSIX.** The Windows app packaging format used by Teams, Slack, Claude, Codex and Manus on this audit's machine.
- **NATS.** A messaging system used between Verevon services.
- **NIP.** Nostr Implementation Possibility: a numbered specification of the Nostr protocol (used by Buzz).
- **OCR.** Optical character recognition (reading text from images).
- **OTLP / OTel.** OpenTelemetry protocol and project, a standard for traces, metrics and logs.
- **OTP.** One-time password (the code sent by email or an authenticator app).
- **PII.** Personally identifiable information.
- **PTY.** A pseudo-terminal, used to run an interactive shell for an agent.
- **PWA.** Progressive web app (a website that installs and works like an app).
- **REA.** The reverse-engineering toolkit used in this audit to analyse the installed apps. In provenance tags, [REA] means a fact observed through REA on a shipped app.
- **RPC.** Remote procedure call.
- **S3.** Amazon's object storage API, also offered by compatible stores such as MinIO.
- **SCIM.** A standard for provisioning users and groups from an identity provider.
- **SEA.** Node.js single executable application: a Node program packaged as one binary.
- **SFU.** Selective forwarding unit: a media server that relays audio and video in group calls.
- **SIEM.** Security information and event management (a central security log and alerting system).
- **SPA.** Single-page application.
- **SRI.** Subresource integrity: a hash that lets a browser verify a loaded script.
- **SSE.** Server-sent events: a one-way stream from server to browser over HTTP.
- **SSO.** Single sign-on.
- **SSRF.** Server-side request forgery: tricking a server into fetching an internal address.
- **STT / ASR / TTS.** Speech to text; automatic speech recognition; text to speech.
- **TLS.** Transport Layer Security (encryption of network connections).
- **TTL.** Time to live: how long something is kept or valid.
- **UI / UX.** User interface; user experience.
- **VM / microVM.** Virtual machine; a very small, fast-starting virtual machine used for sandboxing.
- **WASM.** WebAssembly, a portable binary format that runs in browsers and sandboxes.
- **WS.** WebSocket, a two-way connection between browser and server.
